# Backlog

Items deliberately parked so the work can stay on code ([ADR-0062](adr/0062-code-first-work-mode.md)). People and outside actions are in the [playbook](playbook.md).

This file is the single version of what is pending. The owner, 2026-10-23: "a single version of what's pending. the route map artifact should be based on it in the future". The Route Map is generated from it, never kept apart from it.

## Plan to all clear

The owner, 2026-10-23: "I want an updated backlog that includes what's already there (based on which the route map was built) and anything missed from the reviews. and we make a plan and start knocking things off. you know you have context compression amnesia so use Git to maintain critical memory please. And make sure adversarial checks are done for chunks of codebases that deserve it. not for every nit et cetera. I am so tired of this repo. Things keep moving but nothing moves to all clear ever"

This section is the working memory of whichever agent runs the session: read it first at the start of every session and after any context compression, and update "Where we are" in the same commit as the work it reports. Nothing here repeats a row: each step names the rows and checklist lines it settles.

**All clear** for this phase (Claude's definition, for the owner to correct): (1) byway has had its first live runs with real keys, C4 on Balanced and then C3 with its image, and their ledgers match the providers' dashboards; (2) every line of the Launch checklist below, through "After the first live runs", is ticked; (3) every line of the Review checklist below is ticked, or its row waits on a later milestone (distribution, the study registry, hosted, others using byway); (4) no row says it is due before the first live run. Every other row waits for its trigger and is not part of all clear.

**Checks** (the owner's words above; Claude's reading in ADR-0107's Later note of 2026-10-23): an adversarial check, with refuters, for a chunk of code that touches money (the gateway, the spend book, prices), keys and security, the engine and workflow, or stored data; then branch CI, then the merge. Records, docs, tests-only and scripts-only changes run the gate (`pnpm verify`, or `pnpm exec vitest run scripts/test` for records) and are merged without one.

**Steps, in order**

1. **Records after block 2** (branch fix/fx-b2-after): the sweep's filings, the Review checklist, this plan, the Route Map's backlog half. Check: none (records). Then main's CI.
2. **Route Map v4**, republished at its link from this file's rows at main. From then on the map is generated from this file by a script (built in step 3).
3. **The follow-up branch** (code; landed on 2026-10-28 as fix/fx-landnow, with every other land-now row, under the owner's goal of that day). Check: one adversarial check on its money, security and engine parts: (a) an adapter error's cause and stack redacted before Temporal stores them, with a test that plants a key in a cause (row "An adapter error's cause reaches the engine's history unredacted" (B-026)); (b) the second extract-frame job after a takeover (row "Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs" (F03 (e))); (c) the two fal code notes (row "Block 2's second re-check: two code notes for the follow-up branch" (B-119)); (d) one more try at starting Temporal's server, in byway's own engine start and the test harness (row "Runner tests: Temporal's local test server sometimes starts too slowly under load" (B-117)); (e) a person's own draft or claim-log correction that holds a SEED string asked again, not failed (row "A person's own draft or claim-log correction that holds a SEED string fails the run instead of being asked again" (B-048)). Gate only: (f) the remaining wall-clock limits made time-free (row "The CI time row's listed wall-clock limits made time-free on their own branch, before the first live run" (B-120)); (g) the "changed by" link check (row "A check that each Later note headed by a later record has that record's "changed by" link" (R3 (a))); (h) the fired-triggers script (row "Each block's records pass checks fired triggers and the builders' parked notes" (B-091)); (i) the check-agent rules and the tripwire in AGENTS.md, and `.playwright-mcp/` in .gitignore (done on 2026-10-23, ahead of the branch, so that a session started elsewhere has them) (row "Check agents write into the main checkout: a browser tool's folder and a refuter's marker file" (B-088)); (j) the Route Map generator (done in the backlog sync of 2026-10-23: scripts/route-map.ts); (k) fetch.ts's comment (row "fetch.ts's comment on fal's bounds contradicts ADR-0114" (B-097)); (m) the renderer tries a screenshot once more when Chromium answers "Unable to capture screenshot", the Linux CI flake that failed two runs in a row (row "Known Linux flake: Chromium could not take a renderer test's screenshot" (B-118)), under the check, since the renderer is a security boundary; (l) the reading of row "When a Later note may carry a changed decision, and when it needs a new ADR" (B-084); and the ponytail audit's batches A to F (section "Ponytail audit of 2026-10-24", docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md), each its own commit with its failing test first, under the same check (BUILD-3's script fix, gate only): A, a pause during a step that meets its budget, and the gate question with no retry (B-135, B-136, B-137; fires B-107); B, Clear on a call that is being sent again (F03 (f), B-138, B-139; fires B-038); C, a cancelled attempt sends its call again (F03 (g), B-140, B-141); D, an image bought twice (B-142, with B-045; fires B-044); E, a Start sent again (B-143); F, starting byway on Windows (B-144, B-145, B-146; fires B-087). Batch A changes the workflow hash, so the merge waits for the read-only check that no run is queued, running, waiting or paused. Then branch CI on both machines, the merge, main's CI.
4. **The owner's steps before the first live run** (the Launch checklist's first group: spending limits at each provider, the key rotation, OpenAI's organisation, the keys in .env.agents, a small workspace budget).
5. **The first live runs**, with the owner: C4 on Balanced, then C3 with its image; the price-table check at that session's start.
6. **What the runs settle**: the Launch checklist's groups for those runs and "After the first live runs", and the Review checklist lines they close. Then all clear, by the definition above.

**Where we are** (2026-10-28): steps 1, 2 and 3 done. Under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 ("complete all backlog items the agent can land right now without requiring any intervention from me ... do a single adversarial check as on the complete landed set of rows towards the end. two iterations and report before a third"), fix/fx-landnow landed 97 of the 101 land-now rows (14 builders, one adversarial check over the whole set, one fix round and its re-check; then, the re-check having found that the fix round's cancel rule made a fal clip bought twice, a third round at the owner's "Third round: frame jobs go on (Recommended)", with its own re-check and verifier: a cancelled attempt that still holds its row finishes the made clip's frame jobs, and every try of a frame job asks the spend book first); B-060 and F01 (a) were put to the owner on 2026-10-29, since each changes what byway does: B-060 closed, with its screen change filed as B-173, and F01 (a) landed as a notice at run time (de0515e); X-VAL-4 (a)'s static scan landed after the merge (Semgrep, reporting only). F01 (f) closed on 2026-10-29 at the owner's "B now, C later": the reading stays as ADR-0098 names it, the first live C1 runs count seeds that write no direction, and the stand-in note is B-174, triggered by that count. On 2026-10-29 the checklists' lines whose rows had landed were ticked, each with what closed it (six Launch lines, nine Review lines), and four partly done lines say what is left. Next: step 4, the owner's steps before the first live run (the Launch checklist's first group).

## How this file is kept

The owner, 2026-10-23: "nothing in this artifact should be "artifact only" . everything has to flow from backlog file in Git"; on IDs, "wherever some umbrella code is already available, we add a parenthetical to preserve the umbrella code and at the same time distinguish the component rows"; and on priority and trigger type, "they should be columns because that helps make the Git files readable too no?". The sync of 2026-10-23 put every pending row into this form, and `scripts/test/backlog-format.test.ts` holds it in the gate.

Every pending item is one row of a table with the columns below, in this file (its sections other than this one, the Plan, the two checklists and "Done since the route map of 2026-10-18") and in docs/playbook.md's "1. People and roles", "2. Outside actions" and "3. Milestones". The Launch checklist and the Review checklist stay checklists, and each open line names the row IDs it settles. A row that closes leaves its table for the Done table, with where it landed.

| Column | Values |
|---|---|
| ID | `<code> (a)`, `<code> (b)`, … for a row under an existing umbrella code (the external review's findings F01-F20, release actions R1-R5, RS, review gaps G01-G25 and recommendations X-… and SUB-…; the owner's decision codes D1, D2, …; a Study 1 code the row's title names, such as ST9 or B-BoN); else `B-001`, … in this file and `PB-01`, … in the playbook. Unique across both files. ADR numbers and check finding ids are not umbrella codes. |
| Item | The row's title. |
| Status | `open` or `partly`. |
| Priority | `high` (needed for this phase's all clear, or it protects money, keys or stored data), `medium` (a real fix or record whose trigger can come soon), `low` (parked, rare, or waiting on a later milestone). |
| Milestone | One or more, the main one first: `first-live-use`, `others-use` (before anyone besides the owner uses byway with real keys), `linux-desktop`, `agent-layer`, `hosted`, `macos`, `distribution`, `study1-stage0`, `pilot`, `confirmatory`, or `none` (a follow-up that waits only for its trigger). |
| Who | `agent`, `owner` or `both`. |
| Land now | `yes` (an agent can do it today: no decision or action of the owner's, nothing it waits on open, nothing it needs missing), `small choice: <the owner's one choice>`, or `no: <what it needs that is not there today>`. |
| Size | `S`, `M`, `L` or `XL`. |
| Kind | `fix`, `build`, `test`, `docs`, `chore`, `measure`, `research-decision`, `owner-decision`, `outside-action`, `parked`. |
| Trigger type | `now`, `event`, `other-row`, `owner-decision`, `milestone` or `date`. |
| Trigger | When it is picked up, in at most 30 words. |
| Waits on | The IDs of the rows it waits on, or `—`. |
| Details | The row's text: what, why, the owner's words where there are any. |
| Files | The files or records it touches, or `—`. |
| Filed | The day it was first filed. |

The Route Map is generated from this file and the playbook, and holds nothing of its own: `node scripts/route-map.ts <out.html>` writes the page, and prints its partition (rows in all = closed + open; open = land now + small choice + not now). A records pass that adds, changes or closes a row keeps these columns, and the Route Map is published again from the merged file. The map drawn by hand before this sync, with its own data, is kept as it was in [adr/evidence/route-map/](adr/evidence/route-map/).

## Launch checklist (real runs with real keys)

What only a real run can settle, and what must be done before one, gathered from the rows below, the ADRs and block 2's designs on 2026-10-22 at the owner's request ("file it somewhere so that it surfaces to me for checks otherwise we will forget it. Maybe a Launch Checklist within Backlog."). Before each run of a new kind (the first live run, the first image, the first C5 run, the first run on Best or OpenAI), the agent running the session goes through that group with the owner (block 2 adds this to AGENTS.md, the file coding agents of any provider read); a person can follow it too. Tick an item when it is done and settle the rows it names. "Owner" items are the owner's to do or decide; "Agent" items are work any coding agent can do. A check that confirms a reading byway already relies on says what it confirms.

### Before the first live run

### The first live run (C4 on Balanced)

- [ ] **Owner.** Run C4 on Balanced first. It makes no image and no OpenAI call; with every key it is estimated at $0.07 to $0.46, with a default budget of $1. Then run C3. Hold C5, and C2 on Best or OpenAI, until their rows are settled. C4's text calls go through byway's own fetch (packages/gateway/src/providers/fetch.ts), so if C4's first call fails at the connection, check that fetch first. C4's Anthropic calls are also the first real calls to ask for thinking display "summarized". It confirms: C4 is the only Balanced configuration whose high estimate is under $1. No real provider call has gone through block 2's fetch yet. Anthropic accepts display "summarized" and bills it the same (ADR-0114 item 4 and Consequences, from Anthropic's public thinking page). *Where:* byway Run screen; the server log. *Settles:* "First live run with real keys and real money" (X-VAL-4 (b)) (run order); the waits design's fetch check.

### The first run that makes an image (C3 on Balanced)

- [ ] **Owner.** Run C3 on Balanced and answer Yes to its header image. It makes one Gemini image; with every key it is estimated at $0.27 to $1.62, with a default budget of $2.50. Confirm that the image comes back through byway's own fetch. It confirms: an image takes about a minute, so this run shows only that the fetch works. It cannot show that the old five-minute cap is gone; the local test shows that. *Where:* byway Run and Result screens; the server log. *Settles:* "First live run with real keys and real money" (X-VAL-4 (b)); ADR-0114 live check "The lifted image caps" (Gemini).
- [ ] **Owner with the agent.** Compare the Gemini image call's tokens and cost in the ledger with Google's usage records. Check three things: the 2,000 tokens planned per image; the split between image and text tokens; and whether the thought images, which Google does not charge, are counted. It confirms: byway charges non-image answer tokens at the text rate. If Google bills them at the image rate, byway counts low, by about $0.014 an image at a 630-token gap. A blocked answer may be counted twice (high). *Where:* the ledger; Google's usage and billing records. *Settles:* "Gemini image thinking tokens" (B-028).
- [x] **Agent.** Before the first run that makes an image, keep an image whose wait ran out in the step's memo, as a decline is kept, so that an asset step run again (after a lost worker, for example) does not ask for it again. It confirms: since block 2's fix round an image whose wait ran out ends its slot's tries within one run of the step (MONEY-1, ADR-0114 item 12), but the memo keeps answers and declines, not failures, so a step run again whole would ask again, and Google charges for a call that is stopped. *Where:* packages/runner/src/activities/memo.ts, asset.ts. *Settles:* "An image whose wait ran out can be asked for again if its whole asset step runs again" (B-045). *Done (ticked 2026-10-29):* B-045 landed on 2026-10-28 (fix/fx-landnow, merged at 7499567).
- [ ] **Owner with the agent.** Note what C3 did with its header image: whether it was cut at Trim or kept, and what advice the AI gave. Also note whether the run ended on an image that was billed but not kept, or kept the image beside the piece. A kept image is not on the Result screen; find it under "Open the run". It confirms: the B+ wording of C3's image question (ADR-0101) was chosen without live data. *Where:* byway Run and Result screens. *Settles:* "Measure how often C3's generated header is cut at Trim, and how often the AI adds advice" (B-076); "Images kept beside a prose or page piece are not on the Result screen, and no test shows either screen" (B-047); "An image billed but not kept ends the run" (B-044); "C3's advice: the text gaps ADR-0104's later note records" (B-078).

### The first live C5 run

- [ ] **Agent.** Before the first C5 run, build three C5 items. Sample a middle frame of each clip, best effort and counted in the estimate. Fix the anchor frames' slots knob and wording. Apply the media admission rule to the start frame's upload URL, or record why not. It confirms: the first-live-run row holds C5 until these rows are settled. *Where:* packages/gateway/src/providers/fal.ts; runner; prompts; catalog. *Settles:* "C5 clip evidence: sample a middle frame of each clip" (F10 (a)); "C5 anchor frames: the slots knob is ignored on media, and the prompt says every still is its clip's start frame" (F12 (b)); "The start frame's upload is outside the media admission rule" (F17 (a)). (2026-10-29: F10 (a) and F17 (a) landed on 2026-10-28, fix/fx-landnow, merged at 7499567; F12 (b) is left, after the owner's choice in its row.)

It confirms: fal's fallbacks are on by default, so a clip may come from an endpoint, and at a price, that byway did not choose, while the ledger shows the endpoint asked for. *Where:* owner question. *Settles:* "fal may serve a clip from another endpoint: its fallbacks are on by default" (B-024); "Should an anchor-frame check run before the relay chain?" (F10 (b)).

Also look for a clip byway ledgered as made whose job fal shows as failed (R-MONEY-1's limit, ADR-0114 item 12). If fal's dashboard shows them, note each clip's time in fal's queue beside the start deadline byway sent with it (falStartTimeoutSeconds in packages/gateway/src/providers/fal.ts): a clip that began running after its start deadline means fal honoured neither header, so tell the agent, who asks fal which one its queue reads. Note too each clip's making time beside the margin the deadline leaves below the wait (5, 10 or 15 minutes by clip length; the 15 for clips over 10 seconds is the answer-waits research's scaled figure, which the research says overstates; Claude's margins, ADR-0114 item 6), for the row "Check the answer waits against live call times" (B-103) (the sweep of 2026-10-23; trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23). It confirms: fal accepts X-Fal-Request-Start-Timeout and x-fal-request-timeout. A clean run shows that fal accepts them, not which one it honours. *Where:* fal's dashboard (request history and billing); byway's ledger (request ids). *Settles:* ADR-0114 live check (fal's start-deadline headers); ADR-0114 item 6 (which header fal honours; the start deadline's margins); "Paid-call residuals named only in ADRs" (F03 (e)) (two frame jobs, a clip cancelled twice, a stopped clip fal answered as failed and byway settled as made); "fal may serve a clip from another endpoint".

Likewise, if a stop comes as fal finishes a clip and fal answers that the job failed, byway counts the clip "may have been billed" at its estimate, with nothing in the ledger (block 2's second fix round, R-MONEY-1): check in fal's dashboard whether fal charged for that job, then clear it in Settings' spending records. It confirms: a clip fal drops at its start deadline (504, X-Fal-Request-Timeout-Type "user") is not billed. That rests on two fal pages read together (the queue page and the pricing page), and neither says it outright. For a failed job stopped as it finished, fal's pricing page charges nothing for a request that fails with a status of 500 or more, and a refuter of block 2's re-check read fal's FAQ as saying a 422 may still be charged, so byway counts it as may have been billed, not as nothing and not as made. *Where:* fal's dashboard, by the job id byway records; byway Settings spending records. *Settles:* ADR-0114 item 6, and item 12's R-MONEY-1; backlog "A clip fal drops at its start deadline is counted as "may have been billed"" (answered by block 2). *Rows:* X-VAL-4 (d).

## Review checklist (the external review of 2026-10-08)

Every item of the external review that the trace of 2026-10-18 (at main 8401820) left open with no row saying when it is picked up, each pointing at the row that carries it now and saying when it is picked up: the findings' residuals, the recommendations it found filed without a trigger, and the acceptance items it found still open in F01, F02 and R3. The review's other items are closed, decided or recorded out of scope, or were already in a row with a trigger at that trace and stay in their rows; the Route Map's review view and [the verification](reviews/2026-10-08-external-review-verification.md) trace them. An item closed since that trace is ticked, with what closed it. The owner asked on 2026-10-23: "I don;t know how you will do it but I want an updated backlog that includes what's already there (based on which the route map was built) and anything missed from the reviews. and we make a plan and start knocking things off." and then "a single version of what's pending. the route map artifact should be based on it in the future". So this backlog is the single version of what is pending. Claude filed this list on 2026-10-23, on main 65825f4 with the records pass after block 2's merge, checking each item by hand against the rows, the section "Done since the route map of 2026-10-18" and the commits; a trigger an item's row lacked is marked as Claude's in that row. How it is kept: a records pass that closes or changes a row ticks or updates its line here. 69 lines: 45 open, 24 ticked.

### Findings (F01 to F20)

- [x] **F01** The output ports of audit, a one-shot gate and each were defined twice, with no executed test that they agree; this was also the review's acceptance item "tiny accepted-graph execution tests ... so inferred and actual ports/effects agree". *Closed:* 9d403f8 (R-PORTS-TEST) and ec8b418 (R-PORTS-MOVE), merged with block 2 at 65825f4 (in "Done since the route map of 2026-10-18").
- [x] **F01** Runner.start and the workflow do not check the product rules (no pick, keep-or-revert or scored-loop exit after VOICE) or LS1, so a recipe that reached Runner.start another way would run without them; this is also what is left of the acceptance items on direct runner starts and on a run-time guard for restores. *Row:* "Rules checked only at compose and web start" (F01 (d)). *When:* before any second entry point to Runner.start (a CLI run command, the Claude Code and Codex channel, a hosted API). *Closed:* F01 (d), fix/fx-landnow, merged at 7499567, in "Done since the route map of 2026-10-18".
- [x] **F01** An answer a writer applied can be replaced before delivery by a from-scratch writer or a later pick. *Row:* "An answer applied by a writer can be replaced before delivery by a from-scratch writer or a later pick" (F01 (b)). *When:* before composed recipes are offered to anyone besides the owner, or before the study registry's first entry (Launch checklist). *Closed:* F01 (b), fix/fx-landnow, merged at 7499567, in "Done since the route map of 2026-10-18".
- [x] **F01** An admitted answer can go unapplied on paths no one chooses. *Row:* "An admitted answer can go unapplied on paths no one chooses" (F01 (a)). *When:* before composed recipes are offered to anyone besides the owner, or before the study registry's first entry (Launch checklist). *Closed:* F01 (a), a notice at run time at the owner's "Tell the person at run time (Recommended)", merged at de0515e, in "Done since the route map of 2026-10-18".
- [x] **F01** The claims and answer-reach oracles behind ADR-0097 and ADR-0098 were not in the repository's suite. *Closed:* the claims oracle runs in scripts/test (24e69f5), and the decision evidence was brought in from Temp (b015077, d754402, 24e69f5, 126025f), both in "Done since the route map of 2026-10-18"; the oracle's header says that an answer which never reaches the work is refused by ST12, which the validator's and the advisor's tests guard. When to run it at full size is the row "When to run the claims oracle at the measurement's size" (B-061).
- [ ] **F01** C1-H's seed build is handed the fixed voice deck its prompt never shows. *Row:* "C1-H's seed build consumes the voice deck its prompt never renders" (F01 (e)). *When:* before Study 1's prompt registration (the research track).
- [x] **F01** Influence through candidates is not followed, so such an answer is refused (conservative). *Row:* "Influence through candidates is not followed" (F01 (c)). *When:* before composed recipes are offered to anyone besides the owner, or before the study registry's first entry (Launch checklist). *Closed:* F01 (c), fix/fx-landnow, merged at 7499567, in "Done since the route map of 2026-10-18".
- [ ] **F02** Run inputs travel by value, so a large one can pass Temporal's limits and leave a run in "running"; this is the root byte fix the verification named, which the trace counted as an open acceptance item. Block 2's P2 moved the brief, the copy deck, the draft, the tells, the seeds and the held voice to references (ADR-0113); the texts the models write and the person's reactions still travel by value. *Row:* "What still travels by value after P2" (F02 (c)), with "Activities receive only the ports their step reads (P2's last part)" (F02 (b)). *When:* before composed recipes are offered to anyone besides the owner, when a run is seen stuck in "running" after a long reaction, or when the engine's limits are raised (Launch checklist).
- [ ] **F02** A run that hits a Temporal limit is not mapped to a failed run. Since block 2 (R-RECONCILE, f550a22 and f2d8527) a workflow Temporal closes is caught while the engine runs; one Temporal keeps running while its task fails is not. *Row:* "The lost-run pass catches only workflows Temporal has closed" (F02 (a)). *When:* when a run is seen stuck in "running" or "queued" while the engine runs, or with the row "What still travels by value after P2" (F02 (c)).

- [ ] **F03** When a call's holder dies between settlement and its memo save, the next attempt buys the call again, and both are counted. *Row:* "A counted re-buy when a call's holder dies between settlement and its memo save" (F03 (d)). *When:* a live run's ledger shows one call bought twice, or a third ask (Launch checklist).

- [ ] **R1** The verification's CI design cached Playwright's browser and the Temporal CLI by exact version; it was not built, and CI downloads both on every run. *Row:* R1 (a), The CI proposal the owner deferred ("Not now", 2026-10-18), the larger machines and the CI cache. *When:* when the larger machines are revisited (Windows still red on timing after the time-free branch), or when the owner next raises CI time (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23).
- [x] **R3** The open-decisions history lacked outcome pointers and a link to Deferred decisions (with the acceptance item on index prose that predated ADR-0061). *Closed:* dca74a7, which closed the row "R3 remainder: outcome pointers for decisions already taken".
- [x] **R3** The index's count line was not test-checked (the verification's check (a)). *Closed:* 37b0f56: scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts checks the count line against the index's rows and the record files.
- [ ] **R3** Supersession pointers are only partly done, since nothing checks a missing "changed by" link (the root cause the verification named, a structured and checked relation, and the acceptance item on supersession pointers, both left partly done). *Rows:* "A check that each Later note headed by a later record has that record's "changed by" link (was "Structured "Changes" and "Later records" rows in ADRs, with a back-fill")" (R3 (a)), with "Structured "Changes" and "Later records" rows in ADRs, with a back-fill (optional)" (R3 (b)). *When:* the check on the follow-up branch, due now (the owner's choice, 2026-10-23 08:58; Launch checklist); the optional row if a missing link to a change a record names reaches main despite the check, or when a reader needs each record to carry its own pointers.

The weekly CI run's report was built in batch C of the first block (17e2599): scripts/playwright-pin.ts runs on the weekly schedule only, on the Linux job, with continue-on-error, and says in the log, the run's summary and a warning whether the pins in packages/runner and apps/web have fallen behind npm's latest release; it fails nothing (ADR-0110, ADR-0103's Later note of 2026-10-21). It has not run on GitHub yet: its first output is the first Monday run after the block reaches main, and a run started by hand skips it. Run by hand on 2026-10-21, it said 1.63.0 is pinned and 1.64.1 is the latest, and that 1.64.0, the first minor after the pin, came out on 2026-10-07, so the two weeks are up; the first update still waits for the owner's first live run and the generators' baseline, as the owner chose. Only a record's Context and Decision are final (docs/adr/README.md), so edit the sentence in place, for example to "every test of the report is given fixed pins: the report() tests through a temporary folder, the pinReport() tests in memory", and name the re-check of block 1's fix round (C-PIN-TIMEBOMB) as where this was found. ADR-0110 is the record the first update follows, and a check that reads it against the tests will flag the sentence again. | B-024 | fal may serve a clip from another endpoint: its fallbacks are on by default | open | medium | pilot | both | small choice: send fal's x-app-fal-disable-fallback header on every job, or not | S | owner-decision | owner-decision | Put to the owner when the follow-up branch is briefed; built before the first live C5 run, and in any case before the first study run. | — | fal's reliability page (read 2026-10-22 for the answer-waits research, docs/adr/evidence/reports/answer-waits-research-2026-10-22.md, section 7, Parked) says fallbacks, which reroute a request to "an equivalent endpoint" when the primary fails, are on by default, and that the `x-app-fal-disable-fallback` header turns them off for one request. The page does not say whether queue jobs are rerouted, or how the endpoint that served a request is reported or billed. byway sends every fal job, the C5 clip and its extract-frame alike, through submitJob in packages/gateway/src/providers/fal.ts. Its only headers are Authorization, Content-Type and Accept, so fallbacks are on for both jobs. Then these may be false for fal: ADR-0102 decision 1 (fal's job "is submitted to the endpoint the profile names"), its Consequences and the README's "The model a profile names is the only one that answers", protocol v1.1 §5.1 ("server-side fallbacks are off") and ADR-0038's fallbacks-off rule. fal reports no served model, and the adapter records the endpoint asked for, so a clip from another endpoint would show in the ledger as the one asked for, and the row "Served-model check in the product" could not catch it. The research parked this in the row "Selectable answer waits per model (block 2)", but block 2 did not build it (its plan, WAITS-CAPS), and that row closed with block 2. Decide whether byway sends the header on every fal submission. Off means a clip whose primary endpoint fails is a plain failed job, unbilled by fal's pricing page, and the clip is lost. On means a clip may come from an endpoint and at a price byway did not choose. If the header is sent, add it in submitJob with a test that pins it, as OpenRouter's `allow_fallbacks: false` is pinned, give ADR-0102 a dated Later note correcting decision 1's fal sentence, and confirm at the first live C5 run, in fal's dashboard or request history, that fal accepted the header and which endpoint served each job. If it is not sent, correct ADR-0102, the README sentence and the note against protocol §5.1 to say fal may reroute. Put the choice to the owner first, since it changes what fal may serve. The cheapest moment for the code was block 2's edit of fal.ts for the start deadline; that edit went in (2a60931, 6a1a1ae) without this header, since the choice had not been put to the owner. Block 2 merged (65825f4, 2026-10-23) with no answer. If the owner answers before the follow-up branch lands, the header can go in with that branch's edit of fal.ts (R2-MONEY-1, row "Block 2's second re-check: two code notes for the follow-up branch"); otherwise it needs a change of its own before the first C5 run. Put the question to the owner when the follow-up branch is briefed (the sweep of 2026-10-23; trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23). Pick up before the owner's first live C5 run, with the other rows due then ("C5 clip evidence: sample a middle frame of each clip", the start frame's upload, "Confirm fal's live media host set"), and in any case before the first study run (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-22) Asked on 2026-10-23 at 13:44 whether byway should send the header on every job, the owner answered at 13:54 with a question: "Can the fall backs be decided by the user at the moment of failure?". fal's own fallback cannot: it happens inside fal, within one request, and the header is set only when the job is sent. byway can, with fallbacks off: a clip that fails comes back as a plain failure, and byway asks the person then whether to try the same model again, use another model at its price, or stop. Asked at 13:54 how it should go (fallbacks off now and that question filed for C5; both now; or keep fal's fallbacks), the owner sent a message instead of an answer at 13:57, about the plan's step 3. | B-028 | Gemini image thinking tokens | partly | high | first-live-use | both | no: waits for the first live image run (C3 on Balanced) and Google's usage records | S | measure | event | At the first live run that makes an image (C3 on Balanced), checked against Google's usage records. | X-VAL-4 (b) | Charged since B1 (ADR-0109). Google's pricing page (read 2026-10-18, and again 2026-10-21) prices gemini-nano-banana-2.1's output at "$7.50 (text and thinking)" per 1M tokens on top of the image, and its image guide says thinking tokens are billed. google.ts counts thoughtsTokenCount, which Google's reference counts apart from the answer ("prompt + tool-use prompt + thoughts + response candidates"). It also counts, as the answer's text-rate tokens, the larger of the non-IMAGE tokens candidatesTokensDetails lists and candidatesTokenCount less its IMAGE tokens (since the check of B1, MONEY-2). Still open, each to be checked against the first live image run's ledger and Google's usage records (that run is a Balanced C3, whose image comes from this model; row "First live run with real keys and real money") (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18): (1) Google gives no count per image, so the 2,000 tokens the estimate plans for each image are Claude's planning number. (2) The split relies on the shape of candidatesTokensDetails, which Google's reference describes only as "List of modalities that were returned in the response". A third party (docs.apiyi.com, on gemini-3-pro-image and gemini-3.1-flash-image) found it listing only IMAGE, with candidatesTokenCount 88 to 630 tokens above it. byway charges those tokens at the text rate, so if Google bills them at the image rate ($30 per 1M), byway counts low, by about $0.014 an image at a 630-token gap. If the IMAGE tokens are more than one 1K image's 1,120 (that page shows 2,240 in one answer), byway still charges one image. (3) Neither page says whether thoughtsTokenCount includes the interim "thought images" the guide says are not charged; if it does, byway's cost leans high. The same third-party page reports a blocked answer's candidatesTokenCount equal to its thoughtsTokenCount, which byway then counts twice (high) | gateway (prices.ts, providers/google.ts) | 2026-10-08 |
| B-029 | OpenAI cache writes: check them against OpenAI's usage records | open | high | first-live-use | both | no: waits for the first live OpenAI call (C3's gpt-6-astra critic) and OpenAI's usage records | S | measure | event | At the first live run with an OpenAI key (Balanced's other-family critic, gpt-6-astra): compare each call with OpenAI's usage records. | X-VAL-4 (b) | Since the check of B1 (MONEY-1, ADR-0109), the OpenAI rows record the 30-minute cache-write rate their pricing page lists (gpt-6-astra $12.50, gpt-6.1-sol $2.50, gpt-6-luna $0.125 per 1M tokens, 1.25× input), and providers/openai.ts reads prompt_tokens_details.cache_write_tokens. Before, a written token was charged at the input rate, a fifth below its cost. OpenAI's prompt-caching guide (read 2026-10-21) says caching "is enabled by default for supported OpenAI models" and that cached_tokens "excludes tokens charged as writes". byway sends no prompt_cache_options, so OpenAI writes any prompt of 1,024 tokens or more that it has not cached. No real answer has checked this: the field and its meaning come from the installed SDK (openai 7.28.0: "The unadjusted number of prompt tokens written to cache") and the guide. The check of B1 cited a community thread (2026-07) that showed cached_tokens plus cache_write_tokens above prompt_tokens, which OpenAI called an accounting bug since fixed (the thread is on community.openai.com; the check's agent cited it, and neither Claude nor the money builder read it). byway does not clamp them, so such an answer would be charged for both, which leans high. Claude's alternative, not taken for now (ADR-0109, Alternatives): send prompt_cache_options with mode "explicit" and no breakpoints, which turns caching off for the request, so no token is written at 1.25× input and none read at 0.1× (0.05× on gpt-6.1-sol); decide it from the same run's split of read and written tokens. At the first live run with an OpenAI key (Balanced's other-family critic is gpt-6-astra), compare each OpenAI call's ledgered cost with OpenAI's usage records, and check that cached_tokens plus cache_write_tokens never exceeds prompt_tokens (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-21). So every gpt-image reservation and estimate plans about 18 to 23 times the likely count: about $0.24 an image against about $0.01. A run budget can then refuse an OpenAI image it could pay for, and the estimate shown leans high. | B-033 | A brief larger than a model's context window: no check before the provider refuses it | open | low | others-use | both | small choice: refuse a brief past the smallest context window at Start, or leave it to the provider's 400 with plain wording | M | owner-decision | milestone | Before anyone besides the owner runs byway with real keys, or before a live run with attachments over about 200,000 characters of Chinese or Japanese. | — | Today a very large brief stops at Temporal's limits before anything is spent. Once block 2 stores the brief once and passes its id (P2), it runs, and every step that shows the problem slot puts the whole brief into its prompt (problemSlot, packages/prompts/src/slots.ts). Start accepts 20,000 characters plus five attachments of 200,000 (apps/web/src/server/schemas.ts). The gateway's planning estimate counts non-ASCII text at its UTF-8 bytes (packages/gateway/src/estimate.ts), so a maximal Chinese or Japanese brief is estimated at about 3M tokens, and its real size can reach or pass a model's context window. Nothing in the gateway, the advisor or Start checks a prompt against the window; providers/anthropic.ts only reads model_context_window_exceeded as a cut-off answer. The provider is expected to refuse with a 400, which byway reads as an unbilled invalid request, not retried (errors.ts), and the step fails. A brief well over the window fails at the first step that shows it; before that, a reservation that large may ask "Raise the budget by $X?" for a call the provider then refuses. A brief just under the window can pass its first steps, which are paid for, and fail at a later step whose prompt adds drafts and critiques. No test shows this with a real provider: P2's acceptance runs in demo mode, and block 2's plan names it as a risk (section 5, risk 2) without building anything for it. On 2026-10-21 at 23:05 the owner chose "Approve, and price long-context tiers in W" rather than the design's "Refuse such briefs on live runs". That answer was about the price tier, not the window, so handling the window is the owner's call. The options: refuse, at Start or before a run's first paid step, a brief whose planning estimate passes the smallest context window among the profile's models; or leave it to the provider's refusal, test that refusal with a real key, and word the failure so the person knows the brief was too large for the model. ADR-0113's Consequences name it, with a pointer to this row. Decide it before a live run with attachments over about 200,000 characters of Chinese or Japanese, or before anyone besides the owner runs byway with real keys, whichever comes first (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-22) | apps/web (server/schemas.ts, Start), gateway (estimate.ts, profiles.ts, errors.ts), advisor (estimate.ts), prompts (slots.ts) | 2026-10-22 |

| F02 (b) | Activities receive only the ports their step reads (P2's last part) | open | low | others-use | both | small choice: build it now as a workflow-bundle change, though the owner's answer of 2026-10-21 23:05 left it on its row until its trigger | L | fix | event | When the engine's limits are raised, when a recipe meets the batch bound, or with the row "What still travels by value after P2". | — | P2's root fix had three parts: store the brief once and carry its id, read the seeds from the store, and hand each activity only the ports its step reads. Block 2 built the first two, with the copy deck, the author's draft, the tell catalog and the held voice by id beside them (ADR-0113 items 1 to 4). It left this part on its row. Asked on 2026-10-21 at 23:04 "P2 (design file 2): build it as designed, as Block 2? Its four choices, with my recommendations: row 104, clear a declined critique; W does not start the content-layer pilot (my narrow reading of its trigger); leave 'each step gets only its ports' on its row; leave long-context pricing to batch 0's row.", the owner chose at 23:05 "Approve, and price long-context tiers in W", which took that recommendation. So every activity is still handed its step's whole entry ports. The design's option "Build it in W" read "each step is handed only the ports it reads, through an accessor that refuses a port it was not handed, so a gap fails loudly. About one more day, touching every activity", and its recommendation named the risk "a step silently missing a port it reads, your held words included". It changes the interpreter, so it is a workflow-bundle change, and runs waiting at the upgrade end (ADR-0078). What still travels by value is the row "What still travels by value after P2". Pick it up when the engine's limits are raised or when a recipe meets the batch bound (the design: "Handing each step only its ports is picked up when the engine's limits are raised or when a recipe meets the batch bound"), or with that row | runner (interpreter.ts, every activity), contracts | 2026-10-22 |
| F02 (c) | What still travels by value after P2 | open | low | others-use | both | small choice: the go-ahead for a workflow-bundle change now, before its trigger (the agent says how it reads the content-layer row's trigger when it proposes it) | M | fix | milestone | Before composed recipes are offered to anyone besides the owner, when a run is stuck in "running" after a long reaction, or with the P2's-last-part row. | — | Since block 2's P2 (ADR-0113), a run's brief, copy deck, author's draft, tell catalog and VOICE answers travel by their files' ids, and its SEED strings stay in the store. Every step's input still carries by value the text the models write and the person's reactions in the brief, taste and critique ports, and beside them the option lists and views, which scale with the recipe's shape (ADR-0113 item 11). The answer route caps each reaction at 100,000 characters (apps/web/src/server/schemas.ts). In the catalog the widest fan-out after one is three branches (C5's concepts, C6's audit), so at most about 0.9 MB (the P2 design). A composed recipe could run 32 steps in parallel after one (COMPOSED_LIMITS' peak, packages/recipe/src/limits.ts). At about 300 KB a reaction (the design's 0.9 MB over three branches), that is about 9.6 MB, past Temporal's 4 MB scheduling batch (Claude's arithmetic from the design's numbers, not measured). The engine's batch bound (ENGINE_LIMITS.batchBytes, 2 MiB) counts option ids and views, and leaves the other half of Temporal's 4 MB for these texts (its comment), so it would not refuse such a recipe. A fix could store a reaction as a file and pass its id, as P2 does for VOICE answers, or count these ports in the bound (Claude's). The design said W's record files this as a new row. Pick up before composed recipes are offered to anyone besides the owner, when a run is seen stuck in "running" after a long reaction, or with the row "Activities receive only the ports their step reads (P2's last part)" (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-22) | runner (interpreter.ts, activities/human.ts), recipe (limits.ts) | 2026-10-22 |
| B-040 | After Cancel, Google's calls and images keep running for up to their wait, and are paid for | open | low | first-live-use, others-use | both | no: waits for a live ledger showing a Google call or image settled after its run's Cancel, or for a provider to offer a stop that bills less | S | parked | event | When the live ledger shows a Google call or image settled after Cancel, when Google or OpenAI's image API offers a cheaper stop, or before others use real keys. | — | Cancel cuts off Anthropic's, OpenAI's and OpenRouter's streams and fal's jobs, but not Google's text calls or any image call: Claude's choice in the true stop (ADR-0111 items 3 and 4), whose recommendation read "the others are billed in full anyway", and Google's SDK says an aborted call is still charged. After Cancel such a call runs on, is paid, and its cost is added to the run. Block 2 lengthens how long that can be: a Google text call now waits up to its model's wait, at most 60 minutes, and an image up to 20, where Node's fetch used to give a Google call up after about five minutes (ADR-0114, Consequences). Asked on 2026-10-22 at 17:41 "On Cancel, uncut calls (Google text and images) keep running until they answer, which with your waits can be up to 60 or 20 minutes. Should the run wait for them?", the owner chose at 18:53 "Keep today's behaviour (Recommended)", whose description read "The run shows Cancelled at once; streams and video jobs are cut off; uncut Google and image calls finish in the background, are paid, and their cost is added to the run. Waiting would save no money, since those calls are paid either way." That answer settled whether the run waits for them. No row said when to revisit what they cost (the sweep of 2026-10-22, B2); the playbook's review row lists leaving them uncut as Claude's choice. What could change it: a stop on the provider's side that bills less, if Google or OpenAI offers one. Revisit when the first live runs' ledger shows a Google call or an image settled after its run's Cancel, with its cost; when Google's API or OpenAI's image API offers such a stop; or before anyone besides the owner runs byway with real keys (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-22) | gateway (providers/google.ts, providers/openai.ts), ADR-0111 | 2026-10-22 |
| F03 (c) | Run start: reopen a run whose workflow never started (P3 double fault) | open | low | none | agent | no: needs a reliable "never started" marker on the run record, which byway cannot get while the engine is down (Claude's reading in the row; ADR-0109, "A late start") | M | build | event | If a Start is seen to return a failed run after the engine was down. | — | A Start refused because the engine was down, whose refusal was lost and which is pressed again, gets the failed run back; the next press is a new Start. Runner.start could reopen a named run that failed only because its workflow never started, given a reliable "never started" marker on the run record (ADR-0099). B1 (ADR-0109) built the describe-first check, which this row was to be picked up with, but not this reopen, and records no reason (the first double fault "is unchanged"). Claude's reading: the describe gives no such marker here, since with the engine down it cannot reach Temporal either, and a describe that finds no workflow can be followed by a late start (ADR-0109, "A late start"). | B-043 | A start refused for a conflict leaves a stored brief no run uses | open | low | hosted | agent | no: waits for byway to get a sweep of unused stored files, which it does not have, or for a change to the run-id check in client.ts | S | parked | event | When byway gets a sweep of unused stored files, a person reports the data folder growing, before a hosted byway, or at a change to client.ts's run-id check. | — | ADR-0113's Consequences ("Storage") name two limits that had no row. (1) The brief and the person's own inputs are stored before the run exists (packages/runner/src/client.ts, storeRunInputs). A start that names an existing run id with another brief is refused (RunStartConflictError) after its brief file is written, so one file stays in the data folder that no run uses. Claude called it harmless, like references uploaded for a run that never starts; byway deletes neither. (2) A run whose id were "seed", or began "seed/", would share the seeds' memo key range (seedMemoKey, packages/runner/src/activities/writes.ts). That needs no work: the runner's client refuses any run id that is not a lowercase UUID (RUN_ID in client.ts), and a lowercase UUID cannot begin with "s" (Claude's reading, 2026-10-23). Low priority. If byway ever clears unused files from its data folder, include such briefs and unused references; if the run-id check is ever relaxed, look at the seeds' key range again. Pick up when byway gets a sweep of unused stored files, when a person reports the data folder growing, before a hosted byway (row "A hosted byway: a URL other people can open"), or at any change to the run-id check in client.ts, whichever comes first (the sweep of 2026-10-23, m-37; trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23) On 2026-10-28 client.ts changed on fix/fx-landnow (F01 (d)'s product rules, B-041's describe bound), but not its run-id check, so its trigger has not fired. | runner (client.ts, stored-inputs.ts, activities/writes.ts), store (artifacts, memos) | 2026-10-23 |
| B-044 | An image billed but not kept ends the run | open | medium | first-live-use | both | no: waits for a live asset step that ends this way (C3's note on the Launch checklist); stopping is kept on purpose until then (ADR-0090 §4) | M | parked | event | If a live asset step ends because byway could not keep an image, a declined image's outcome or the placing answer, or at the next change to billedStop or memo.ts. | — | Kept on purpose: a storage or ledger fault could repeat, so the step stops rather than buy again; the images it already made are stored but not in the work. Recording the slot as failed and going on as a partial result is the alternative (ADR-0090 §4). Revisit if a live run ends this way (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18). ADR-0112's Consequences (2026-10-21, "Claude's findings, not acted on") accept two more cases on the same path, and no row named them until now. (1) A declined image whose outcome byway cannot keep. memo.ts keeps a final decline with a plain StepError, because a decline is not always billed. That error is neither a billed GatewayError nor a PaidNotKept, so asset.ts's billedStop never sees it: the run's failure names no slot, and does not say that the images already made were billed and are not in the work. The closed row "A paid image whose result byway cannot save fails without naming its slot" fixed the same gap for made images. The decline's record carries its cost (DeclineRecord.costUsd, retryCostUsd), so a fix could give only billed declines the slot sentences. (2) The placing answer's own completion cannot be kept. askModel for helper.asset.integrate throws PaidNotKept, and the integrate catch in asset.ts handles only ContractMissed, so the step fails with every bought image outside the work, the outcome ADR-0112 removed for a placing answer that misses. Keeping the work unchanged with the images beside it, as for ContractMissed, may not help, because the store fault that stopped the memo would likely stop storeText and stepFinished too. Both need the memo store to fail four saves (about 45 s) right after the call. memo.test.ts tests (1) at memo level only, and no asset-step test covers either case. Revisit them with the rest of this row: if a live run's asset step ends because byway could not keep a declined image's outcome or the placing answer, or at the next change to billedStop or to memo.ts's errors for a result it cannot keep (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-22) On 2026-10-28 its trigger fired (memo.ts changed on fix/fx-landnow, rows B-142 and B-045): case (1) is done in code there, so a billed decline that byway cannot keep names its slot. The row's main question and case (2) wait, as before, for a live asset step that ends this way. | runner (activities/asset.ts billedStop, activities/memo.ts) | 2026-10-09 |

C2's default run budget (a quarter above the high estimate, rounded up) is $3.50 on Best and $2.50 on OpenAI, and 12 boards cost about $2.90 by byway's estimate, so a live run that opts in with most of its 12 options left after dedupe runs out at the boards and asks for more money; on Balanced, 12 boards cost about $0.59 (about $0.41 before B1 charged a Gemini image's text and thinking, ADR-0109). The decision brief (2026-10-16) recommends boards for the chosen options only. Its skeptic adds three points. Find the chosen options from something VOICE cannot edit (an optional 'chosen' flag in the option map, or the map as the option-map step wrote it), not from the 'Chosen.' that opens a rationale, which VOICE offers for rewriting. Cap the boards at 3, with a note naming any chosen option left without one (nothing enforces 1 to 3 today). Say "the chosen options" in the explainer. Held by the owner (2026-10-17). Deferred because it is a larger UI change: present.ts would show each step per place it runs, or across stages, in place of one entry per step object. The catalog configurations C1 to C7 and C6a are not affected (the claims B-060 widened on 2026-10-28, where they hold on every path, stay). If you look back at the original source on lenny's newsletter, it doesn;t invlve such cimplicated proceedings. design is design." (ADR-0107). Have the page-building step keep reader-facing copy in marked slots (a content layer the design renders), so VOICE edits the slots and "exactly as typed" holds by construction, and show the person the rendered page to confirm before it is kept (the human check the source workflow relies on). This would replace ADR-0081's browser arbiter (the visitor read, geometry, and its CSS limits) with something far simpler; it needs a new ADR, a page-format change in the web-page prompts and the runner, and a migration note for runs under the old format. Held by the owner (2026-10-17). The decision brief's skeptic (2026-10-16) advised keeping the arbiter as it is and putting no more work into it (no round 15). When a trigger fires, run a pilot first: about 5 real C1 runs through the whole pipeline, roughly $6–30 on Balanced by byway's own estimate. The triggers: an arbiter fix is needed, a line the person needs is not offered, a refusal no rewording fixes, or a workflow change is made for another reason. The pilot counts, mechanically, the lines today's question leaves out and whether copy marks survive the critic, image, CUT and swap rewrites; a quality comparison by an AI judge would need a Program Registry entry (CR3) first. Then choose between the content layer (with a fallback that offers any unmarked text as its own line), a source-text editor, or retiring only the arbiter and adding the person's look at the page. The content layer's work list also misses attribute copy (alt, aria-label, placeholders, button values, the meta description). Any Study 1 deadline is the prompt registration and freeze, not the study registry's first entry, and C1-H's parent hash has been pinned since 2026-10-18 (c1h.ts, C1H_PARENT_HASH), so a change to the product's C1 no longer moves C1-H's hash, which removes that deadline. A crashed worker's calls hold their run's budget for up to 60 s, so a step retried within that time can be refused at run scope (ADR-0095). The verify() cache misses an edit that keeps a file's size and modification time, until the next append, and CJK briefs reserve more input, so fewer of their calls run at once (ADR-0091). Revisit any of them when a live run or a person's report shows it (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18). A run start that failed is marked failed once a describe finds no workflow; a start that lands after that describe (the failed start itself, or another delivery's of the same run) finds the run failed, and between the describe and that store write a call could begin. A connection reset after Temporal accepted a start, with the describe failing too, marks the run failed with "Nothing was spent" though its workflow exists. A workflow left under a run marked failed so, when its recipe asks a question first (C4, C6, C7), opens that question and waits, unseen and spending nothing, until byway's workflow code changes: the next engine start after such an update terminates it and withdraws its question (retireStaleRuns), while a restart without one leaves it, since the start-up reconcile leaves ended runs alone (ADR-0109). Revisit these if a live run's ledger shows a call charged to a run whose error says it did not start or that byway cannot tell whether it started, or the engine is seen holding a running workflow for a run History shows as failed (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-21). Since the first block's fix round (2026-10-22, RS-2; ADR-0111's Consequences), a step that heard a timeout with its run going keeps reading the store and cuts its call off once the run ends; after a timeout the next attempt resumes an accepted fal job by its request id, so when the run then ends both attempts send fal's cancel for that one job. fal's queue page does not say what a second cancel answers. Each answer acts on the one row by the state it finds, not by when it arrives: a made clip settles it; a 200 "removed" releases it, clearing a "may have been billed" mark the other attempt's answer set; a 202 "requested" or a failed cancel marks it "may have been billed" only while it is still open, sent or accepted. So a 202 and a 200 for one job count nothing, whichever came first, and if fal billed that job the budgets count low (Claude's reading of the spend book's `settle`, `release` and `abandon`; the re-check of the fix round, F2-RC-1). Check it with the fal readings in the row "Check the billing assumptions that no provider documentation backs", if a live run's ledger shows a clip cancelled twice; if fal billed such a clip that byway released, make one attempt's release leave a row the other attempt marked "may have been billed" (triggers proposed by Claude, 2026-10-22). The re-check of the fix round did not trace a resumed fal clip that reaches its last-frame job; Claude traced it on 2026-10-22, with two findings. (1) The frame job's cancel answer never acts on the row. A stop at that stage settles the row as a made clip, at the clip's price plus the frame job's bound (fal.ts runJob for 'extract' and its stopped branch; gateway.ts video's record). A settle overrides any other state, and release and abandon cannot undo it (spend-book.ts), so two attempts cancelling different frame jobs on one row count correctly. (2) Both attempts can submit a frame job. Suppose an attempt that heard a timeout keeps polling the clip, and the next attempt resumes the row a lease later with only the clip job listed. When the clip finishes, each attempt submits its own extract-frame job, since the first attempt's submit does not check the book once the call is marked sent (paid.ts markSent returns early). So fal bills two frame jobs, the row counts one bound (about $0.002), and the book keeps only the later job's id. This contradicts ADR-0091's "The same holds for the last-frame job." If it is fixed, a call checks before each later submission that it still holds its row, and stops without submitting if another attempt has taken the call over; if it is left, ADR-0091 gets a dated Later note saying that sentence does not hold under a takeover. Pick it up when a live run's fal dashboard or ledger shows two extract-frame jobs for one relay clip, or at the next change to fal.ts's runJob or resume handling or to paid.ts's markSent, whichever comes first (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-22). That trigger fired in block 2, with neither done: 2a60931 changed fal.ts's resume handling (a resumed clip keeps the time fal accepted it) and bounded the last frame by the time left on the clip's wait, and block 2 changed paid.ts's resume beside markSent, which still returns early once a call is marked sent (ADR-0114 items 6 and 7). So two attempts can still each submit a last-frame job, and ADR-0091's sentence has no note yet (said here in block 2's records pass). Pick it up when a live run's fal dashboard or ledger shows two extract-frame jobs for one relay clip, at the next change to markSent or to the last-frame job's submission, or before C5 is offered to anyone besides the owner, whichever comes first (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-22). That trigger fired in block 2's fix round: c19155f changed the hook every fal submission goes through, so that a call that becomes resumed as it is marked sent submits nothing (MONEY-3). A call already marked sent never reaches that check, since markSent returns at once, so two attempts can still each submit a last-frame job. Block 2's check found that ADR-0091's note was owed (RECORDS-3), and ADR-0091 now has it (its Later note of 2026-10-23); the Launch checklist's item on it is done. Pick it up when a live run's fal dashboard or ledger shows two extract-frame jobs for one relay clip, at the next change to markSent or to the last-frame job's submission, or before C5 is offered to anyone besides the owner, whichever comes first (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23). Three more from block 2's second fix round, named only in ADR-0114 item 12 or a builder's notes until the sweep of 2026-10-23. (1) Since R-MONEY-1 (14749fc), fal's own failure answer to a stopped clip's look counts the clip as may have been billed. ADR-0114 item 12 records a limit of it as Claude's reading, "a rarer case recorded rather than fixed": once the stop is set, the poll's own result read drops fal's failure answer and leaves the look's read to decide, so if fal answers the first read with its failure and the look's read then meets a lost connection, the clip settles as made. Its price, about $0.35, then goes into the append-only ledger and the run's cost, for a job that fal's pricing page does not charge (a status of 500 or more). Pick it up when a live run's ledger shows a stopped clip settled as made whose job fal's dashboard shows as failed, or before C5 is offered to anyone besides the owner, whichever comes first. The follow-up branch changes falFailure and stoppedGeneration's comment (row "Block 2's second re-check: two code notes for the follow-up branch"), and its records pass says here whether that change touches this case (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23). (2) Since R-MONEY-2 (aed7b78), seenDone gives up a clip's status read after 30 seconds, but the read and its retries may run on in the background, ignored, because fal's client takes no abort signal for them (ADR-0114 item 12, "Claude's, not widened"). They are free reads, so the cost is open connections and log lines, not money. (3) After untilEnded gives up a clip's status poll (at its wait or at the stop), withRetries may send the abandoned subscribeToStatus again with its aborted signal. The real SDK rejects at once, so this is at most a few extra free reads, and it was true before block 2 too. Block 2's fix-round money builder left it for the coordinator to file or ignore, and neither was done until now. Revisit (2) and (3), at low priority, if a server log shows fal status reads running on after their call ended, when @fal-ai/client takes an abort signal for status and result reads, or at the next change to seenDone, pollJob, untilEnded or the gateway's attempt hook, whichever comes first; naming (3) in ADR-0114 would take a dated Later note (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23) On 2026-10-28 (fix/fx-landnow, the check's MONEY-1) the second extract-frame job after a takeover, the plan's step 3 item (b), is no longer submitted: before each submission after a call's first, the gateway asks the spend book whether this attempt still holds the call's row (SpendBook.holds), and the attempt that lost it submits no last-frame or middle-frame job, so fal bills the frame jobs once and they are counted (ADR-0091's Later note of that day). The other residuals above are unchanged. | gateway, runner, store | 2026-10-18 |

| B-079 | Check the billing assumptions that no provider documentation backs | partly | high | first-live-use | both | no: waits for the first live runs' ledger, and settles each reading only if its event happens | M | measure | event | Settle each reading from the first live runs' ledger against the providers' usage records when its event happens; the long-context tiers at the first call past their threshold. | X-VAL-4 (b) | errors.ts treats a provider-bodied 5xx and a mid-stream error event (overloaded_error, api_error) as unbilled, which lets billingOf() permit a blind re-send (at most one attempt) (ADR-0091, ADR-0095). ADR-0102 records OpenAI's image moderation block at no cost, because OpenAI reports no usage with it. Documentation read again on 2026-10-18 (ADR-0109): only fal says server errors are not charged ("Server errors (HTTP 500+) are never charged"); the error and pricing pages of Anthropic, OpenAI, Google and OpenRouter say nothing about failed requests. Google's billing page does: it says a request that fails with a 400 or 500 error is not charged, and says nothing about timeouts or aborted calls (read for the answer-waits research of 2026-10-22, docs/adr/evidence/reports/answer-waits-research-2026-10-22.md, section 3). So the reading of a provider-bodied 5xx as unbilled is documented for Google too. ADR-0109's Later note of 2026-10-22, "Google's billing page", records it, and errors.ts's comment now gives it (the page read again on 2026-10-22). OpenAI's image guide now documents moderation_blocked (error.type "image_generation_user_error", with an optional moderation_details.moderation_stage of input, output or unknown, which the stored error body keeps). It names neither content_policy_violation nor the HTTP status, and does not say whether a block is billed. If either is billed, budgets count low. Since the true stop ([ADR-0111](adr/0111-cancel-cuts-off-the-calls-byway-can-stop.md), 2026-10-21), a mid-stream error event is read as unbilled on Anthropic's streams only, and these readings join the list, none of them documented by the provider: what a stream that byway closed bills on Anthropic, OpenAI and OpenRouter (byway counts it at its reservation as "may have been billed"; none of the three says, for its own API, whether a closed stream is billed for its input only, for what it generated or in full, and OpenRouter's streaming page says its abort stops billing for the providers it lists); fal's 200 to a cancel, read as a job fal removed before it ran, which counts nothing (Claude's reading of fal's queue page and its FAQ); an error chunk after the 200 in OpenAI's and OpenRouter's streams, or a stream with no finishing chunk, read as "may have been billed" (sent once more at most, with that attempt retained); and a stream that finished without its usage chunk, settled at its planning figures as an assumed price. Since the first block's fix round (2026-10-22): an error chunk's own code is not read, so an upstream 429, 503 or moderation refusal that OpenRouter sends as an error chunk, once its `: OPENROUTER PROCESSING` comments have committed the 200, counts as "may have been billed" and is sent once more with that attempt retained, while the same refusal as an HTTP error before the 200 is read as unbilled and retried with backoff; OpenRouter's streaming page does not say when those comments commit the response (TS-MONEY-4, left as is for the first live run). A stream that sends nothing for its model's whole wait is read the same way, "may have been billed" and sent once more at most (CC-2; ADR-0114 item 4). Until block 2 that was fifteen minutes, or forty-five in an image or video step, and on main Node's fetch closed a silent OpenAI or OpenRouter stream after five minutes, read the same way (ADR-0114, Context). And after a stop, an error whose own reading is unbilled (the provider's error answer) is now read by that error and the call released, as it is without a stop (TS-MONEY-5), so the provider-bodied-error assumption above covers that race too. Since block 2, a call that uses its whole chosen wait is counted as "may have been billed" at its reservation, under the reason "waited" (ADR-0114 item 8; packages/gateway/src/waited.ts and the spend book's abandon). It counts against the workspace cap until the person clears it, but not against its run's caps (ADR-0095). Its readings join the list. A Google text call or a Google image stopped at its wait: Google's SDK says an aborted call is still charged (GenerateContentConfig.abortSignal, as the approved waits design reads it, and as ADR-0111 reads it for Cancel), so such a call is billed while its run's caps do not count it. An OpenAI image stopped at its wait: OpenAI documents nothing about it. A fal clip cancelled at its wait: fal's 200 counts nothing, as above, and a 202 or a cancel that failed counts as "may have been billed"; nobody documents whether cancelling a running Kling job stops its billing (the research, section 6). And a clip fal drops at the start deadline byway sent with it (a 504 whose X-Fal-Request-Timeout-Type is "user") is read as nothing billed and released (ADR-0114 item 6, Claude's reading): fal's queue page says such a job gets that 504, and its pricing page charges nothing for a request that fails with a status of 500 or more and nothing for queue time, but no page says in one sentence that a dropped job is not billed. Since block 2's second fix round, a clip job that fal answers as failed when a stop comes as it finished counts as "may have been billed" at its estimate, with nothing ledgered (R-MONEY-1, the reading the owner's option of 2026-10-23 06:25 named; ADR-0114 item 12): fal's pricing page charges nothing for a status of 500 or more, and a refuter read fal's FAQ as saying a 422 may still be charged. Settle them from the first live runs' ledger against the providers' usage records; that shows each only if a 5xx, a mid-stream error, a moderation block, a cut-off call, a fal cancel, a stalled stream, a stream without its usage, a call that used its whole wait or a clip fal dropped at its start deadline happens. Two more readings join the list (the sweep of 2026-10-23). A provider-bodied 4xx: errors.ts reads a 4xx (a 400, a 413, a 429) as unbilled (ADR-0091 Decision 1). Only Google's billing page documents this, for a 400; Anthropic, OpenAI and OpenRouter say nothing (ADR-0109's Context, "Failed requests", and its Consequences). Since block 2's P2 a brief past a model's context window runs, and is expected to get such a 400 (ADR-0113's Consequences; row "A brief larger than a model's context window: no check before the provider refuses it"). If a 400 is billed, budgets count low. Settle it from the first live runs' ledger if such a 4xx happens, or when that row's provider refusal is tested with a real key (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23). And how the long-context thresholds are counted (LC-TIERS, 5d07fe3; ADR-0115 item 1, Claude's choices): byway counts the whole prompt, cached tokens included (Usage.inputTokens), compares strictly (">"), and reads "272K" and "200k" as 272,000 and 200,000 tokens (packages/gateway/src/prices.ts, LongContextTier.aboveInputTokens). Neither OpenAI's page nor Google's says how its threshold is counted. Each reading leans high, so the cap holds, but if one is wrong byway records such a call above its cost. Calls that tell the readings apart: one whose uncached part is under the threshold, and one between 272,000 and 278,528 tokens (272 × 1,024), or between 200,000 and 204,800. Compare the tier and the charge the provider billed with the ledger at the first live call whose prompt, counted with its cached tokens, passes 272,000 input tokens on an OpenAI model or 200,000 on Gemini 3.1 Pro, a brief of very roughly 200,000 characters or more (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23) | gateway (errors.ts, waited.ts, providers/openai.ts, providers/stream.ts, providers/fal.ts, prices) | 2026-10-22 |

| R3 (b) | Structured "Changes" and "Later records" rows in ADRs, with a back-fill (optional) | open | low | none | agent | no: the owner chose (b) for now and filed (a) as optional; waits for its trigger | L | docs | event | If a missing link to a change a record names reaches main despite (b), or when a reader such as a paper reviewer needs each record's own pointers. | — | Optional, at the owner's words of 2026-10-23 08:58: "(b) for now. File (a) as a backlog item and mark it optional there (but provide this context in the backlog filing)". The context the owner asked for. **What (a) is.** A "Changes" row in each new record naming the records it changes, a matching "Later records" section on each record it changes, and a back-fill of every existing relation, all checked by scripts/test/adr-records.test.ts, so a change a record names in its "Changes" row fails the gate when its index link or its "Later records" entry is missing, whether or not a note was written. It cannot catch a change no record names. ADR-0103 kept them "in the backlog with triggers" and called them a reading aid, since the index's "changed by" links already hold the relations; ADR-0100 notes that corrected records carry no pointer to the records that correct them. The D4 owner-decision row that first named them closed with ADR-0103. **What showed the gap.** The decision-log check cannot see a missing "changed by" link (ADR-0103 §2). A trace of the external review (2026-10-18) found ADR-0087 without its link to ADR-0090, and named ADR-0082 as changed by ADR-0100 item 10, which ADR-0100 does not say. The check of the first block (2026-10-22) found ADR-0110 extending ADR-0103 §3, its weekly Playwright pin step, with no link on ADR-0103's index row (MI-1 / REC-B1-2, blocking; docs/adr/evidence/reports/block1-check-2026-10-22.md), and Claude then found ADR-0110 changing ADR-0105 item 1 with no link and no note yet (7735a91 added both links). Block 2's re-check found ADR-0114 changing ADR-0090 with neither (R-RECORDS-3). **What (b) catches and misses.** (b), the row "A check that each Later note headed by a later record has that record's "changed by" link", would have caught MI-1, whose note was there. It misses a change that has no note yet, the ADR-0105 and ADR-0090 cases. (a) would have missed both too, since in each the later record named the change nowhere: ADR-0110's "What this changes in earlier records" said "Nothing", and ADR-0114 did not mention ADR-0090. (a) adds coverage only for a change its author did name, whose link or "Later records" entry is missing (block 2's second re-check, R2-RECORDS-1, corrected this context on 2026-10-23). **The links written by hand.** Block 2's records pass (2026-10-22) wrote ADR-0113's links to ADR-0081, 0090, 0092, 0096 and 0098, ADR-0114's to ADR-0091, 0095, 0099, 0102 and 0111, and ADR-0115's to ADR-0103 and 0109; the records pass of its fix round (2026-10-23) ADR-0114's to ADR-0081; and that of its second fix round ADR-0114's to ADR-0090. **Why before a merge.** A missing link that lands on main can come later only as a permanent LINK_EXCEPTIONS entry (adr-records.test.ts, adr-log.ts). Pick up if a missing link to a change a record names reaches main despite (b), or when a reader such as a paper reviewer needs each record to carry its own pointers (the first proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23; the last is the D4 brief's). A change no record names is caught by neither (b) nor (a); only a reader who checks the behaviour against older records finds it, as block 2's re-check did | docs/adr, scripts/test (adr-log.ts, adr-records.test.ts) | 2026-10-23 |

| B-095 | undici is pinned at 7.30.1, apart from Node's own copy (was "undici is pinned at 7.18.2 beside Node's own copy") | partly | medium | none | agent | no: waits for the next Node upgrade, an advisory naming undici 7.30, or the advisory step | S | chore | event | At the next upgrade of Node (.node-version), when an advisory names undici 7.30, or with the advisory step, whichever comes first. | — | Since block 2 (e433823; ADR-0114 item 3), the Anthropic, OpenAI, OpenRouter and Google clients fetch through undici's own fetch with one Agent whose timeouts are 80 minutes (packages/gateway/src/providers/fetch.ts). undici is an exact dependency of @byway/gateway. Block 2 pinned it at 7.18.2, the version Node 24.13.1 bundles (Claude's choice), and this row's advisory trigger had already fired when it was written: 7.18.2 had 23 published advisories. Block 2's check found it (SECURITY-1). Its fix round moved the pin to 7.30.1, which none of undici's 55 repository advisories covers, as read on 2026-10-23 (323145c; ADR-0114 item 12). Eight advisories of 2026-10-15 also cover 7.29.1 and 7.30.0 and are not yet in GitHub's global database, so npm audit passes those versions. fetch.test.ts holds the pin at 7.30.1 or later, and a probe of the same shape as the local probe of Node's fetch limits gave the same limits on 7.30.1. What is left: (1) Node's own fetch, which fal's calls and the price report use, still carries the undici Node 24.13.1 bundles, 7.18.2, until .node-version moves. (2) Nothing watches either copy for new advisories (row "Dependency advisories and a static security scan"). At each Node upgrade, check the undici it bundles, keep the gateway's pin at a version no published advisory covers, and run fetch.test.ts and the local probe of Node's fetch limits again (docs/adr/evidence/probes/node-fetch-limits-2026-10-22/). Pick up at the next upgrade of Node (.node-version), when an advisory names undici 7.30, or with the advisory step, whichever comes first (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23). (3) Most of fal's reads have no deadline of their own (ADR-0114 item 3). Four do: the last frame's result read and its download (frameBound), a stopped clip's result look (RESULT_LOOK_MS), and a resumed clip's status read and the look after a failed cancel (CANCEL_TIMEOUT_MS). The start frame's upload (storage.upload) and a finished clip's result read (readResult) keep Node's default fetch, so only the undici bundled with Node bounds each try, at about 305 s without headers or between body bytes (the probe of Node's fetch limits, run again on 2026-10-23); the gateway retries those reads as free reads. A Node upgrade can move that bound. At each Node upgrade, read the probe's result for these reads too, and give them a deadline of their own if the bound moved, or if a live C5 run shows a relay step held up by a fal upload or result read (the sweep of 2026-10-23; trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23) | gateway (package.json, providers/fetch.ts, providers/fal.ts), .node-version | 2026-10-23 |
