Decide whether the README's renderer section tells Linux users to use Noto CJK, or whether the renderer pins its fonts. D2's Linux support (ADR-0105) did not settle it. It also left untested whether Google's server sends its headers early. And before a stream's response begins, the SDKs' own ten-minute request timeout still applies, whatever wait is chosen (block 2's waits design, section 2). A default that is too short closes a call that was still working and sends it once more, so it can be paid twice. One that is too long holds the call's estimate against the budget, and keeps a stuck run waiting longer. What byway records today falls short of an exact comparison. The spend book keeps each paid call's sent_at and settled_at (paid_operations, packages/store/src/migrations.ts), but one row spans every attempt of the call, a resend after a stall included. A call that ends uncertain or released has no settled_at. The stall bound (providers/stream.ts) restarts at each chunk and keeps no record of the longest gap. Protocol v1.1 §12 splits the engineering into two tiers. P0, on the pilot and Stage 1 critical path: the gateway spend gate, ledger and tags; the recipe format and bypass transform; the agent runtime for all arms; the sandbox and mirrors; the render service, logs and failure classifier; the model view; the minimal lay pairwise Judge app with its scheduler, in-browser encryption and validity service; ratings custody; the release script. P1 is built during Stage 1 review. ADR-0059 gives each requirement's "When". Some parts exist from the product (the recipe format, the renderer). The spend gate does not: nothing checks a Program Registry entry before a paid call (ADR-0046), and the gateway has no registry check. Nor do the Judge app, the agent sandbox (ADR-0031) or the Python evaluation side. The protocol's central case assumes 2 full-time engineers. Open inputs: Judge hosting and who needs Lab access (open decisions in the ADR index), the WSL memory cap and sandbox network policy (ADR-0031), and proposed ADR-0039's data layer. Pick up when the owner resumes the research track (ADR-0108; Deferred decisions, "When the research track resumes"). The cost probe (Pricing) needs the registry entry and spend gate, and the pilot (~February 2027) needs P0. B now, C later. But file C with the correct trigger so we end up doing it if needed." The count is the Launch checklist's line under "After the first live runs". Admission reads C1's seed (prompt article.SEED, the only one in DIRECTION_FIRST_SEED_PROMPTS, packages/recipe/src/semantics.ts) as replacing the notes beside the work on every run, but the runner stores the direction as a note only when the model writes two or more lines before its block (directionOf, packages/runner/src/activities/model-step.ts), so on a run whose seed writes none the notes before it stay (interpreter.ts keeps only a returned noteArtifactId). The fix: when a direction-first seed writes no direction, store a short stand-in note ("No direction was written for this page.") and return it, so the notes before the seed are replaced on every run, as admission assumes; then the 55 answers and the 6 that reach only drafts inside candidates (ADR-0098) are refused by construction, and F01 (a)'s unused-answer notice is exact for these seeds. Constraints from the recommendation the owner chose: no extra paid call and no new way for a run to fail; the research channel (C1-H and its ablations, which share article.SEED) left out, so no Study 1 arm changes; and the stand-in stored under its own label, not "<step>: Direction", so it does not spoil the count. It is engine code, so it gets ADR-0107's check. "More than rarely" (1 in 20) is Claude's reading, for the owner to correct. Attribute copy (alt, aria-label, title, placeholder, meta description), `<pre>` content and the other kinds it lists (script-generated copy, CSS content, textarea default text, noscript content and more) are never offered for the person to write; each stays byte for byte. The page lock holds the words and each link's address, but not visibility (the hidden attribute, inline styles, classes) or the spacing and line breaks between lines. So a later machine write that hides the person's line, or runs two of their lines together, passes the lock. Decide with the content-layer question (its work list also leaves out attribute copy), or when a composed recipe puts a web-page writer after VOICE. A Windows job takes 28 to 40 minutes (40 in run 39172144829, on fix/fx-d2, whose browser tests alone took 19.7 minutes) and a Linux job 14 to 18; `pnpm verify` takes about 7 minutes on the owner's machine. The proposal put to the owner, answered "Not now" (its option read "Keep CI as it is; timing flakes get named and set aside."): merge on the local gate; Linux CI on every push to main; Windows CI weekly and by hand; unit and browser tests as parallel jobs; recorded as a new ADR amending ADR-0103. The Windows CI write-up made for the owner on 2026-10-18 (updated that evening after D2 merged; docs/reviews/2026-10-18-windows-ci.md) lists two options the proposal did not: larger GitHub machines (4 or 8 CPUs), much faster and with fewer stalls at a higher price per minute, an organization setting and so the owner's; and reducing the engine's own stalls (row "Engine-thread stalls on slow machines"). It recommends making the limits above time-free soon, in one bounded batch like fx-ci-timing, rather than at the next red Windows run, and revisiting running Windows CI less often only if Windows still goes red on timing after that; no answer is recorded. Not built either: the verification's design cached Playwright's browser and the Temporal CLI by exact version, and CI downloads both on every run. Build that cache, keyed on the Playwright version and @temporalio/testing's (the review's R1 trace), when the larger machines are revisited, that is if Windows still goes red on timing after the time-free branch, or when the owner next raises CI time (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23). Asked at 03:40 "The 'CI time' row also owes you the larger-machines option from the 2026-10-18 Windows CI write-up: bigger GitHub machines for the Windows job, much faster with fewer stalls, at a higher price per minute. It is an organisation setting, so yours. Switch?", the owner chose at 06:25 "Not now (Recommended)", whose description read "Keep the standard machines; the time-free work removes the failures that stalls cause. Revisit if Windows still goes red on timing after it.", over "Yes, I'll set one up" (docs/adr/evidence/conversation-continued.md). The larger machines are revisited if Windows still goes red on timing after that (the owner's "Not now (Recommended)"). | .github/workflows/verify.yml, ADR-0103, GitHub organization settings (owner) | 2026-10-18 |
| B-124 | An interrupted `pnpm e2e` on Windows leaves its engine running | open | low | none | both | small choice: the owner's yes to delete the 31 byway-e2e-* folders (about 57 MB) in the system temp folder; the fix itself needs none | S | fix | event | At the next change to scripts/e2e.ts, or when an e2e engine is next found running; deleting the left folders goes to the owner with the next question. | — | Found on 2026-10-23 at 03:07 local, during the check before block 2's merge that no run is waiting: a Temporal dev server (`temporal-sdk-typescript-1.24.0.exe server start-dev`, on port 59934) started at 00:32 by scripts/e2e.ts's byway server for a byway-e2e-* data folder in the system temp folder, with its parent gone. It was an e2e run cut off during block 2's check: the STORE-1 refuter reported a Temporal dev server it could not stop, on that port (the fix round's web builder's report). Claude stopped it at 03:08. scripts/e2e.ts stops byway and removes its folder in a `finally` block, which a killed parent never reaches, and Windows does not end a process's children with it. 30 byway-e2e-* folders (45 MB) were left in the temp folder. None of it was byway's own data: byway's port 7233 was free and its data folder absent. An engine left so keeps its port and its SQLite file open, and the folders pile up. The fix (Claude's proposal): have the e2e server end the engine when its own parent is gone, and have each e2e run remove the byway-e2e-* folders an earlier run left; or at least say in the README how to find and stop such an engine. Pick up at the next change to scripts/e2e.ts, or when an e2e engine is next found left running, whichever comes first (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23). A count after block 2's merge found 31 byway-e2e-* folders (about 57 MB) in the system temp folder, none holding byway's own data. The fix would remove them only at the first e2e run after it is built, so they stay until then. Deleting them sooner is the owner's call: put it to the owner with the next question, and on a yes the agent deletes them (the sweep of 2026-10-23; trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23). The first, from run 39152075629 (Windows, a browser test that waited too long, the closed row "Known Windows timing flake: browser test C5 waits at most 120 s for each next turn"), shows Temporal's own SQLite store, in the data folder on C:, timing out ("context deadline exceeded") and its calls taking up to 12 s; it cannot show retirements or relaunches (item 4). | CI, docs/adr/evidence/logs | 2026-10-18 |
| B-129 | Engine-thread stalls on slow machines | open | medium | first-live-use, others-use | both | no: waits for a live run or a kept server log that shows stalls, or Windows CI red on timing after the follow-up branch makes the tests time-free | L | fix | event | When a live run or a kept server log shows stalls, or if Windows CI still goes red on timing once the tests' time limits are gone. | X-VAL-4 (b) | The investigation behind ADR-0106 measured pauses of byway's own process of 2.5 to 4.1 s on the owner's machine held to 2 CPUs under load, and up to 9.5 s at start-up under bursts of load. During a pause timers fire late: a close is judged late (since ADR-0106 that costs one relaunch; row "What the late-close fix left", item 1), and fixed waits run out. On CI's Windows machine, Temporal's own SQLite store timed out ("context deadline exceeded") and its calls took up to 12 s (run 39152075629; the closed row "Known Windows timing flake"). Likely sources: the spend book's money transactions commit with synchronous=FULL on the engine's thread (packages/store/src/spend-book.ts); node:sqlite's DatabaseSync holds the thread while it waits out its 10 s busy timeout on the file the web app also writes (db.ts); on CI, the e2e data folder sits under the system temp folder on the runner's throttled C: disk (scripts/e2e.ts); and plain CPU starvation. A person's slow machine (on battery, under antivirus, with a slow disk, in a VM) can see the same. The Windows CI write-up (2026-10-18, docs/reviews/2026-10-18-windows-ci.md) said to keep this, as the one option that also helps slow user machines. Moving the commits off the engine's thread is product work with its own design and checks (the spend book's durability is ADR-0095's); moving CI's data folder off C: is a workflow change. Pick up when a live run or a kept server log shows stalls, or if Windows CI still goes red on timing once the tests' time limits are gone (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18) | store (spend-book.ts, db.ts), runner (worker), scripts/e2e.ts, .github/workflows/verify.yml | 2026-10-18 |

## Ways to run byway (owner direction, 2026-10-17)

Local works today: `pnpm start` runs the app and its engine on the person's own machine, with their keys from `.env.agents` (ADR-0064). A hosted byway was deferred by ADR-0064. The owner named a third way on 2026-10-17: inside Claude Code and Codex, on the person's own subscription. Neither row below is designed or built.

| ID | Item | Status | Priority | Milestone | Who | Land now | Size | Kind | Trigger type | Trigger | Waits on | Details | Files | Filed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B-130 | Run byway inside Claude Code and Codex, on the person's own subscription | open | low | agent-layer, distribution | both | no: the owner has not scheduled the design, and it needs a design brief and an ADR before any build | XL | build | owner-decision | When the owner schedules the design; it needs a design brief and an ADR before any build. | — | The owner (2026-10-17): "there is a third layer which is running byway inside claude code. Maybe via MCP or some other approach. Using claude code's subagents within a session if needed." It matters because people then use their own subscriptions instead of byway reselling tokens. Claude Code and Codex only for now. Not designed: it needs a design brief and an ADR before any build. The design has to settle: how a recipe's model steps run on the host's model (an MCP server, a plugin or skill, or another form; host subagents where a step needs isolation, as protocol/06-runbook.md already does with a design-critic subagent for C1); what becomes of the gateway as the one path out for model calls, and of the spend book, budgets and ledger, when the host's subscription pays (ADR-0030, ADR-0095, spec §6); how the person's checkpoints are asked inside the host; how such runs are labelled, since the host's model is not one of byway's pinned profiles; what each host's terms allow a tool to do with the person's subscription; whether the host can make images and video on the person's subscription, or media steps still need byway's own provider keys, and with them its spend book and budgets (byway buys images and video from other providers' APIs today; open since 2026-10-17); and whether the channel is product only, as the provider and model picker is, since a study pins its models and records them exactly (ADR-0041). Earlier records: ADR-0064 kept the skill/MCP channels for later "on the same engine" and said to revisit the form after first use; the ADR index's open decision on the product's form called the portable layer the cheapest first probe; ADR-0067 lists an agent-CLI executor, once sandboxes exist, as a follow-up. A plugin or server that others install is a distribution (see "Before any distribution"). Pick up when the owner schedules the design | new channel (MCP server or plugin), runner, gateway, advisor, catalog, ADR | 2026-10-17 |
| B-131 | A hosted byway: a URL other people can open | open | low | hosted | owner | no: the owner has not said a hosted URL is wanted; it needs its own design and ADR | XL | parked | owner-decision | Only if the owner wants a hosted URL (owner question: is it wanted, given the Claude Code and Codex layer?). | — | Not built, and deferred since ADR-0064 ("Hosted multi-user deployment, auth, billing and the skill/MCP channels come later"). Today there is no sign-in, byway binds 127.0.0.1 and admits only loopback Host headers (README), and keys come from the server's environment, so every visitor would spend the owner's keys. A hosted design needs its own ADR: sign-in, tenancy, per-person keys or billing, and data residency. It must also revisit choices earlier records made for one local user: a separate renderer process or low-privilege account (ADR-0084: "Revisit if byway is ever hosted or multi-user"), the renderer's OS sandbox as one setting for the whole data directory that the person switches off in Settings (ADR-0105), which in a hosted byway any visitor could switch off for every render on the server, a token on every request against cross-site requests (ADR-0074 found it heavier than a local tool needs), the per-process single flight for the first recommendation (ADR-0091), and the open decision "Who needs Lab access" (ADR index). Owner question: is it wanted, given the Claude Code and Codex layer? Pick up only if the owner wants a hosted URL | apps/web (sign-in), scripts/serve.ts (binding), gateway (keys), store, runner (renderer), ADR | 2026-10-17 |

## Owner decisions from the external-review verification (2026-10-08)

Everything else from [the verification](reviews/2026-10-08-external-review-verification.md) is implemented or scheduled. The owner decided all four on 2026-10-16: D3 (ADR-0101), D1 (ADR-0102), D4 (ADR-0103) and D2 (ADR-0105). All four are built, and their rows have closed; what D2 left is the row "What D2 left (ADR-0105)".

## Before any distribution

Applies to a packaged build or the replication kit (R2). Verified facts: `@img/sharp-win32-x64` 0.35.5 (Apache-2.0 AND LGPL-3.0-or-later) was installed in production, when checked on 2026-10-17, as next@16.3.8's lazily loaded optional image optimizer and, since the lockfile change in 7f8001d (D4, when yaml was added; ADR-0103), also as an optional peer of minimizer-webpack-plugin; byway never imports next/image, and sharp's binary bundles LGPL and MPL-1.1 libraries. Next has been 16.4.1 since 22fb4e1 (2026-10-23), which changed only next and its @next/* packages; its lockfile entry still lists sharp 0.35.5 as an optional dependency. Run `pnpm why sharp -r --prod` again before relying on this fact (the sweep of 2026-10-23; 22fb4e1's body left these facts as they were checked on their dates). `lightningcss` and `lightningcss-win32-x64-msvc` 1.33.0 (MPL-2.0) come in through vitest and vite and, since that change, also in a production install, as an optional peer of minimizer-webpack-plugin under webpack under @temporalio/worker (a dependency of @byway/runner) (checked with `pnpm why <package> -r --prod` on 2026-10-17). Neither loads at run time through minimizer-webpack-plugin: Temporal's workflow bundler runs webpack in development mode, which does not minimize. sharp can load through Next all the same: Next's image endpoint (`/_next/image`) is live, because `apps/web/next.config.ts` leaves image optimization on (Next's default), and Next loads sharp when that endpoint resizes an image (row "Next's image optimiser is on, though byway uses no optimised image"). `caniuse-lite` (CC-BY-4.0) is a production dependency that needs attribution. The Temporal CLI and Playwright's Chromium are downloaded at runtime and carry their own licences. `unionfs` 4.6.0 has no licence field but ships an Unlicense LICENSE file. A plugin or server for Claude Code or Codex that others install would be a distribution too.

## Ponytail audit of 2026-10-24

The ponytail audit of main at e8e9322 (docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md) gave 20 findings and 19 smaller ones. Each was checked against the code under the owner's protocol of 2026-10-24 (ADR-0107's Later note of that day), and the plan the owner approved at 02:48 ("Approve as recommended (Recommended)") is docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md. Of the 45 findings checked, 43 are valid and are the rows below, one row each, except that report items 2, 20 and the isEntry finding were found twice and have one row for both ids. Batches A to F are in the plan's step 3; G to N wait for the triggers in their rows.

Not taken, with the reasons the check gave: **WEB-6** (the dev server's copy of the guard's cross-site rule): the two rules differ when Sec-Fetch-Site is empty, so merging them would loosen the dev guard; a tests-only check that ties the two rules together can ride in batch N. **RECIPES-10** (three copies of the roles that never see the work's source): prompt-shows.test.ts already ties two of the sets, and if the prompts copy drifted, a composed step would be refused at admission, before anything is paid.

| ID | Item | Status | Priority | Milestone | Who | Land now | Size | Kind | Trigger type | Trigger | Waits on | Details | Files | Filed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B-147 | An image, or a Gemini answer, that comes back without usage is recorded as costing $0 | open | high | first-live-use | agent | small choice: settle a delivered answer that has no usage at planning figures, marked assumed? | S | fix | milestone | Before the first run on Best or OpenAI (the Launch checklist), as the plan's batch G, with its own check. | — | Ponytail audit MONEY-3 (report item 16; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch G of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix changes behaviour or a contract (the owner's yes needed). For a streamed chat answer whose usage never arrives, completeStreamed (openai.ts:201) sets the counts to NaN on purpose, so the call is settled at its planning figures and marked assumed, "rather than as free". parseImagesResponse uses `res.usage?.input_tokens ?? 0` and `?? 0` instead. So an Images answer without `usage` is settled at $0.00 with no assumed mark, and GPT Image 2.5 is priced by tokens only. The SDK types that field as optional and its comment says "For gpt-image-1 only" (openai/resources/images.d.ts:343-346). The Google parsers do the same when usageMetadata is missing: a text call settles at $0, and an image at its per-image price only. Example: OpenAI answers gpt-image-2.5-sunburst with {data:[{b64_json}], output_format:'png'} and no usage. The result is costUsd 0, spentUsd 0 and no assumedPricing, so both budgets count a billed image as free and Settings does not mark it. Fix: parseImagesResponse: when res.usage is undefined and an image came back, use `{ inputTokens: NaN, outputTokens: NaN, units: 1 }`. parseGoogleResponse: when res.usageMetadata is undefined and the answer is neither a refusal nor a decline, report NaN counts. parseGoogleImage: when usageMetadata is undefined and an inline image came back, report NaN counts with units 1. finiteUsage then settles the call at its planning figures, marked assumed, as the streamed path does. | packages/gateway/src/providers/openai.ts, packages/gateway/src/providers/google.ts | 2026-10-24 |
| B-148 | C2's suggested run budget is smaller than what its first fan-out reserves, so the run asks for more money at its first step | open | high | first-live-use | agent | small choice: (A) a seed allowance of 8,000 tokens, or (B) a suggested budget that covers the largest fan-out | S | fix | milestone | Before the first C2 run, as the plan's batch H, with its own check. | — | Ponytail audit RECIPES-1 (report item 6; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch H of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix changes behaviour or a contract (the owner's yes needed). The estimate plans typical output (for example 2,000 tokens for an option). It never covers what one fan-out holds in flight at once. C2 starts its 12 seed calls together (replicate runs Promise.all). Seeds are builders, so each gets the 16,000-token allowance. On Balanced each seed reserves about $0.163 (16,000 tokens at Sonnet's $10 per million, plus the prompt). The suggested budget is $1.50 (estimate $0.37 to $1.03), so 9 seeds are admitted and the 10th is refused. The run then asks 'Raise the budget by $1?' during its very first step. If the person says no it ends with 'The run stopped at its budget of $1.50', on a run whose whole planned cost is at most $1.03. On Best the figures are 12 x $0.326 = $3.91 against a suggested $3.50 (10 admitted). C1, C3 and C5 fit, and so do C2 on the OpenAI and Google profiles. Fix: Owner's choice. (A) Smallest: in model-step.ts give role 'seed' on every medium except web-page an allowance of 8,000, not the report's 4,000, because of adaptive thinking. That is about 2 lines. (B) suggestBudget takes the larger of high x 1.25 and (the high-end spend before the largest fan-out plus that fan-out's reservations), using a peak field from estimateRecipe. | apps/web/src/view/format.ts, packages/advisor/src/estimate.ts, packages/runner/src/activities/call.ts, packages/runner/src/activities/model-step.ts, packages/runner/src/workflow/interpreter.ts, packages/store/src/spend-book.ts | 2026-10-24 |
| B-149 | The estimate ignores the critic's shuffles knob and the runner's default number of references | open | medium | first-live-use | agent | small choice: land after the first C4 and C3 runs? | S | fix | event | After the first C4 and C3 live runs, as the plan's batch I, with its own check. | — | Ponytail audit RECIPES-2 (report item 10; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch I of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix changes behaviour or a contract (the owner's yes needed). In the runner, a GREAT critic with at least 3 references makes `shuffles` calls (clamped to 1 to 4), each over the whole lineup. A GREAT critic with no refs knob, or refs 0, takes up to MAX_REFS = 10 references (the web allows 9). The estimate always counts one call, and counts the refs knob as references, else 4 for GREAT; refs 0 gives 0. Cases: C5's launch-gate critic (GREAT, refs 4, shuffles 2) is priced as 1 call per gate run but makes 2, and the gate can run twice with its retry. C2's annotate critic (GREAT, no refs knob) is priced over 5 views but reads up to 10 when the person gives 9 examples. A composed critic loop with shuffles 4 (a registered knob, range 1 to 4) makes 4 critic calls a round where the estimate counts 1. The high end, the default budget and the question figures all come out below what such a run spends. Fix: In the critic case: shuffles = GREAT && knob > 1 ? min(4, floor(knob)) : 1, multiplying the high tally. For GREAT readers use refs > 0 ? min(refs, 10) : 10. About 4 lines. Optionally share one pure counting function with planCritic. | packages/advisor/src/estimate.ts, packages/runner/src/activities/critic.ts | 2026-10-24 |
| B-150 | A gate's retry is priced as re-running only its target stage, while the engine re-runs every stage from that one through the gate's own | open | medium | first-live-use | agent | small choice: land after the first C4 and C3 runs? | S | fix | event | After the first C4 and C3 live runs, as the plan's batch I, with its own check. | — | Ponytail audit RECIPES-4 (report item 11; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch I of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix changes behaviour or a contract (the owner's yes needed). The same rule is written two ways. runStages re-enters stage j and runs every stage from j through the gate's stage, and limits.ts counts exactly that. estimate.ts counts the target stage's base span plus the gate body. C5's gate sits in D3 and retries D2. A retry repeats D2 and then D3's steps before the gate: apply-cut, the tells sweep and apply-swaps (3 model calls), and the person's CUT, swap choice, A/B compare and VOICE again (about 16 to 48 minutes). The estimate's high end leaves all of that out. Fix: Record the stage order and the current stage in the Walk. The retry's span becomes the sum of stageBase over the stages from retry.stage through the gate's stage, as limits.ts does. It double-counts the gate's own high-end retry part, which errs high and is accepted. The interpreter applies the gate right after the critic or check. A passing verdict, or the cap answered 'use', throws LoopExit before the fix or regenerate step after it runs. | B-157 | A provider refusing a recommend or compose request (model not found, request too large, 403) tells the person to try again | open | medium | none | agent | small choice: an invalid-request error answers 400, and the wording for a 403 that cannot be retried later | S | fix | other-row | After step 3 merges, as the plan's batch J, in the order of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md. | — | Ponytail audit WEB-2 (report item 9; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch J of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix changes behaviour or a contract (the owner's yes needed). It has no case for 'invalid-request' without a held key. That is what the gateway returns for any provider 400, 404 or 413: a retired or unknown model id (not_found_error), a request over the context window (request_too_large or 'prompt is too long'), any invalid_request_error. Such an error goes through to handle(), which logs it and answers 500 'Something went wrong inside byway. Try again.' A non-retryable 'provider-error' gets 'The model provider did not answer. Try again in a minute.', for example a 403 such as OpenAI's 'organization must be verified' or Anthropic's permission_error. The provider did answer, and trying again cannot work. The gateway's own message, already redacted, names the cause ('anthropic returned 404: … model … not found'), but the person never sees it. Fix: Step 1, small: in the switch add `case 'invalid-request': return { status: 400, message: err.message };`. Step 2, separate and needing a choice of wording: in `case 'provider-error'`, return `{ status: 502, message: err.message }` when `(err as { retryable?: unknown }).retryable === false`, and make api.test.ts:403's fixture retryable. Add no advice about shortening the brief: that wording is B-033's open owner decision. | apps/web/src/server/model-errors.ts | 2026-10-24 |

It counts days in UTC 'so server and browser agree'. In byway the server is the person's own machine, so the two already share a time zone. For anyone not on UTC, a run that finished 2.5 hours ago on the same local evening reads 'Yesterday', and an older run near midnight shows the next or previous day's date. Fix: Give formatWhen an optional `timeZone?: string` (default: the process's own zone). Count days from the calendar date in that zone: take the date parts from Intl.DateTimeFormat(…, { timeZone }).formatToParts, or getFullYear, getMonth and getDate when no zone is given, and take Date.UTC of y/m/d divided by the day length. Drop the hard-coded timeZone 'UTC' from both formatters. Build them per call when a zone is given. Compare years in the same zone. ## Done since the route map of 2026-10-18

The first 44 are items the route map of 2026-10-18 (drawn at main 8401820) listed as open. The first 34 of them landed on main before 37a4104 and were checked item by item there on 2026-10-22, at the owner's request to mark completed items (2026-10-22 19:29). The route map's data and that check are kept in [evidence/routemap/](adr/evidence/routemap/), whose README says how the check was made. The next ten were built in block 2. Claude added them in block 2's records pass, before block 2 merged, checked against fix/fx-block2 at 1c9c5e3 by their commits and records, not re-checked by a second agent; they reached main with block 2's merge, 65825f4 (2026-10-23). The rest were filed after that map and added to the Route Map in v3 (2026-10-22) or v4 (2026-10-23): the items Route Map v4's classification (2026-10-23, at c751c43, its calls checked by refuters) marks done or decided, two of them settled by the merge itself, and the rows the records pass after the merge closed. Claude added them in that pass, at main 65825f4. Route Map v4 is republished at the same link, with statuses at main 65825f4. Of the 21 items the map had tagged as in Block 2, 18 are done and three partly: "Activity inputs carry the brief and the step's ports by value (P2)" (below), "Rates byway does not record, and a maximal brief passes Google's 200K tier" and "When to run the claims oracle at the measurement's size", which stay in their rows. A row closes, and is removed above, when its work is done; this list keeps the completed items visible.

The review also named a limit of its own (X-COV-2): it did not audit the dependencies' own code or their history, and it asked for no action; nothing in byway audits them either, and the advisory step above would check known advisories only, not the code. pnpm audit, like npm audit, reads only the advisories in GitHub's global database, so it misses those a package's maintainers have published in its own repository and GitHub has not yet added. Block 2 showed this: eight undici advisories of 2026-10-15 covered 7.29.1 (the version SECURITY-1 proposed) and 7.30.0, and npm audit passed both (323145c; row "undici is pinned at 7.30.1, apart from Node's own copy"). pnpm audit also cannot see the undici inside Node, which is not in the lockfile. So for an exact-pinned network dependency (undici, next) the step, or the session's agent, also reads the package's repository advisories, as block 2's check did by hand, and Node's own copy is checked against Node's security releases. | A Cancel and the lost-run pass can both close the same run (B-017) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/runner-lifecycle, 6b28f37), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: The Store contract gains endRun(id, {status: done\|failed\|cancelled, error?, costUsd?, outputArtifactId?}): Promise<boolean>. SqliteStore implements it inside updateRun's IMMEDIATE transaction (a shared private #updateRun with an unlessEnded flag). It changes nothing and returns false when the run has already ended, rejects a status that is not an end status, and throws not-found as updateRun does. settleLostRun ends the run through endRun, still withdraws questions every time, and logs run-failed or run-cancelled only when endRun ended the run. Every caller (client cancel and resolve, versioning, the pass) goes through settleLostRun. lifecycle.ts's finish, fail and cancel are left as they were: they run inside the workflow, in sequence, so they cannot overlap the pass. Dated 2026-10-28 Later notes added to ADR-0113 (its first Later notes section) and ADR-0096. | Row removed (its text: settleLostRun (packages/runner/src/lost-runs.ts) reads the run, sets it failed or cancelled with store.updateRun, and then appends run-failed or run-cancelled. updateRun (packages/store/src/store.ts) writes whatever status it is given, and the two events have different dedupe keys. Since block 2 (R-RECONCILE, f550a22 and f2d8527 on fix/fx-b2-engine), the engine repeats this pass every five minutes. The web app's Cancel calls the same function when Temporal no longer has the run's workflow (packages/runner/src/client.ts). So if a person presses Cancel on a run whose workflow Temporal has just closed, between the pass's read and its write, the run's History shows both run-cancelled and run-failed, and the run keeps whichever status was written last. Before block 2 the pass at start finished before the web app started (scripts/serve.ts), so only two web requests on the same lost run could overlap. The fix is a run update in the store that changes the status only while the run has not ended, and says whether it did. It goes inside updateRun's IMMEDIATE transaction, the Store contract (packages/contracts/src/store.ts) gains it, and settleLostRun appends its event only when that update changed the run. It is unlikely, but a person would see two ends on one run (the R-RECONCILE builder's residual (a)). Pick up when the store's run updates (updateRun or the Store contract) next change, when a run's History shows both run-cancelled and run-failed, or before anything other than the web app and the engine writes to the store (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-22)) |
| The lost-run pass catches only workflows Temporal has closed (F02 (a)) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/runner-lifecycle, 6b28f37), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: workflowRunning becomes workflowState: absent, closed, stuck or running. A workflow is stuck when Temporal says RUNNING and the pending workflow task is on attempt 5 or later (STUCK_TASK_ATTEMPTS). The pass the engine repeats while it runs now (1) leaves alone only a queued run with no workflow, and closes a queued run whose workflow exists and has closed, and (2) terminates a stuck workflow and fails its run with a new STUCK_RUN_MESSAGE, reported in a new Reconciled.stuck list. The engine prints a new line for stuck runs (engineStartLines, EngineStartReport.stuck, optional). The pass at start never counts tries, because they would have been made before the worker polled. The option skipQueued is renamed whilePolling in dev-engine.ts and apps/web/e2e/seed.ts. Later notes are on ADR-0113 and ADR-0096. The threshold is Claude's: measured on the Temporal CLI dev server 1.32, a failing task is tried again about every 10 s, so the 5th try comes after roughly 40 s of nothing but failures. | Row removed (its text: Since block 2 (R-RECONCILE, f550a22 and f2d8527; ADR-0113 item 7.8), the engine repeats its lost-run pass every five minutes while it runs (reconcileWhilePolling, packages/runner/src/dev-engine.ts). It fails a run only when Temporal reports the run's workflow as not running (workflowRunning, packages/runner/src/lost-runs.ts). A workflow that Temporal keeps RUNNING while its workflow task keeps failing (for example, a completion over gRPC's message size) reads as running, so its run stays "running". That may be the batch limit the closed row "Reconcile lost runs while the engine runs, not only at start" and the P2 design named ("A run that Temporal closed on a payload, batch or history limit is then marked failed instead of staying "running"."): if Temporal does not close a workflow at that limit, the pass does not catch it. The repeated pass also skips queued runs, because a start may be under way, so a queued run whose workflow closes before the run begins (beginRun) stays queued until the next engine start. The tests stand in for a limit with a terminate carrying Temporal's history-limit reason (packages/runner/test/reconcile-while-running.test.ts); no real payload, batch or history limit was hit (the R-RECONCILE builder's residuals (b) to (d); the sweep of 2026-10-22, B9). A possible fix (Claude's): read a running workflow's pending workflow-task failures and fail a run whose task keeps failing, and let the repeated pass fail a queued run whose workflow exists and has closed. Pick up when a run is seen stuck in "running" or "queued" while the engine runs, or with the row "What still travels by value after P2" (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-22)) |

| An adapter error's cause reaches the engine's history unredacted (B-026) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/gateway, a619dda, c7ed2f3), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: Fixed the root cause in toFailure (runner/src/activities/failures.ts), which every activity goes through. Any Error that is not byway's own and not a Temporal failure is now turned into ApplicationFailure.create({ type: err.name, message: err.message }). Its stack and its cause chain no longer reach Temporal's history (temporal.sqlite). The type is the error's name, as Temporal's default converter would set it, so the retry policy reads it as before. Temporal failures (such as a cancellation) and values that are not errors are passed on unchanged. redactMessage in gateway.ts is unchanged, so its DOMException-copy edge case (conditional on that function changing) was not triggered. | Row removed (its text: When fal's adapter throws something other than a GatewayError (a fetch TypeError with undici's cause, a DOMException), the video path passes it on through redactMessage (packages/gateway/src/gateway.ts). That function redacts only the error's message, and its cause goes on as it was: the re-check of block 1's second fix round planted a key in a cause's message, and the key survived (B1R2-CODE-6). The runner's toFailure (packages/runner/src/activities/failures.ts) rewraps only byway's own errors (an AnswerRejected, a GatewayError or a StepError), returns an ApplicationFailure as it is, and passes any other error on unchanged. Temporal's default failure converter then writes that error's message, stack and whole cause chain into the activity's failure event, which the engine keeps in its history (temporal.sqlite in the data directory). The stack also keeps the original message if anything read it before the redaction. The run screen is safe, since failureMessage shows only the redacted top message. The history is not, and ADR-0074 says no key is stored. No adapter is known to put a key in a cause today (undici's causes carry network text), so the risk is low, but nothing guards against it. The fix: in toFailure, turn every error that is not a Temporal failure into an ApplicationFailure that carries only its redacted message and its name, which covers every activity, not only video; or redact (or drop) the cause chain and the stack in redactMessage. Add a test that plants a key in a cause and reads the failure the activity hands Temporal. A separate edge in the same function: a frozen DOMException is copied without its internal brand, so reading .code on the copy throws. Nothing reads it today (f361a6f's comment); copy its code as a field of its own if the function changes. That trigger fired in block 2: c5b8910, 2a60931 and 6a1a1ae changed how gateway.ts and fal.ts wrap a call whose wait ran out, a last frame past its bound and fal's start-deadline 504 (ADR-0114 items 5 to 8), and neither redactMessage nor toFailure changed, so a cause chain still reaches the history (said here in block 2's records pass). Pick up at the next change to redactMessage, to any adapter's error wrapping or to toFailure; when byway starts logging or showing raw activity errors or Temporal history; or before anyone besides the owner runs byway with real keys, whichever comes first (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-22). That trigger fired again in block 2's fix round: 0d9a5f0 moved fal's result read and its 504 reading into readResult, and c19155f added the gateway's error for a clip call taken over as it submits; redactMessage and toFailure did not change. The Launch checklist puts this before the first live run, which these triggers did not name (block 2's check, RECORDS-2). Pick up before the first live run (the Launch checklist), at the next change to redactMessage, to any adapter's error wrapping or to toFailure, when byway starts logging or showing raw activity errors or Temporal history, or before anyone besides the owner runs byway with real keys, whichever comes first (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23). It fired again in block 2's second fix round: R-MONEY-1 added fal.ts's error for fal's own failure answer to a stopped clip's look (a cut-off error whose words name the clip job and what fal answered), and R-MONEY-2 made a status read that runs out count as unreadable; redactMessage and toFailure did not change. The work and its triggers stand as above (said here in that round's records pass, 2026-10-23)) |

| guard-sandbox's blind spots (B-036) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/web-server, 991b5f9), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: In guard-sandbox.test.ts, the line reader (now codeLines) removes a leading /* ... */ before the comment test, and reads a line ending in `import` together with the next line (LC-1). Two new pins: every createRequire line (3 imports and 4 call lines), and every require or requireFromRunner(.resolve) call whose argument is not a written-out string (none today) (SEC-NOTE-3). Six new cases must each move a pin: block-comment import, block-comment .launch, split import(, computed load through createRequire, requireFromRunner(computed), require.resolve(name). The test header and the Guardrails section of apps/web/README.md now list what is closed and what is still unseen. ADR-0105 has a Later note dated 2026-10-28 saying its 2026-10-22 note was wrong about the launch-call pin. | Row removed (its text: apps/web/test/guard-sandbox.test.ts lets Playwright, Puppeteer and chromium-bidi be named only in packages/runner/src/pages.ts, apart from two lines of scripts/playwright-pin.ts that only read version numbers. It allows Playwright's launch calls only in pages.ts, and it lists every import() whose argument is not one written-out string (none today). That is what stops a second launch site from going around the renderer's OS sandbox and its scrubbed environment (ADR-0084; ADR-0105 item 7). The test's header and ADR-0105's Later note of 2026-10-22 list ways past it that block 1's fix rounds described but did not close. A code line that begins with a block comment (LC-1): uses() skips every line that begins with //, /* or *, so `/* x */ import { chromium } from 'playwright'` is never read. A `.launch(` line written the same way is hidden too, so ADR-0105's note is wrong that the launch-call pin covers this; give it a dated Later note saying so. An `import` whose `(` is on the next line, with a computed module name. A module loaded by a computed name through a require made by createRequire (SEC-NOTE-3); dev-server.ts, dev-engine.ts and scripts/serve.ts already call createRequire, with literal names. And the older gaps: a namespace import called by a computed name, SQL that builds the setting's table or column name from pieces, a browser driver it does not list, and a browser started as a program of its own. Each needs a deliberately odd layout, so none is likely by accident. The first three are small to close: in uses(), strip a leading /* ... */ before the comment test; join a line that ends in `import` with the next line; pin createRequire's lines, and every require whose argument is not a written-out string, as exact lists; and add each as a case that must move a pin. At the same time, bring apps/web/README.md's Guardrails text up to date: it says the guard leaves out lines that are only a comment, and it names neither the three gaps nor the import() pin. Pick up at the next change to guard-sandbox.test.ts, to the launch wiring in pages.ts or to scripts/playwright-pin.ts; when a product file loads a module or calls a require by a name it builds, or adds a browser driver; or before byway takes a code contribution from anyone besides the owner (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-22)) |

| An image whose wait ran out can be asked for again if its whole asset step runs again (B-045) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/engine-memo, 64e265b, 36c65e6), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: A call that used up its model's wait, and that the gateway does not send again (waitedOf(e) and not retryable), is now kept under its memo key, as a final decline is. A step run again (lost worker, budget raise) gets the same GatewayError back, with its words, its billed/billing and its waited mark, and asks nothing; asset.ts makeImage still reads it as waited. A stream's silence (retryable: true) is not kept, so Temporal's retry still asks again as before. The keep is best effort: if the store fails 4 saves, the step gets the gateway's failure as today, so a step run again would ask again (code comment marks this as a shortcut). A racing attempt's kept outcome wins, as for declines. The ADR-0099 Later note (2026-10-28) records both memo changes. B-044 (1), whose trigger fired on this memo.ts change: a final decline with a recorded cost (costUsd > 0, retryCostUsd > 0 or billed) whose outcome cannot be kept now fails as PaidNotKept, so billedStop names its slot. A decline with no recorded cost stays a plain StepError. Stopping is unchanged. This is recorded in ADR-0090's Later note and the billedStop doc. B-044 itself stays parked: its main question (stop or go on as partial, ADR-0090 §4) and case (2) (a placing answer's PaidNotKept) wait for a live run and the owner, as the row says. | Row removed (its text: Since block 2's fix round (f853b53; ADR-0114 item 12; MONEY-1 of block 2's check), an image whose wait ran out ends its slot's tries within one run of the asset step, since Google charges for a call that is stopped and a resend mostly pays twice (the owner's "Give up and end the call plainly (Recommended)", 2026-10-22 18:53). The step's memo (packages/runner/src/activities/memo.ts, once()) keeps answers and declines, not failures. So when Temporal runs the whole step again after a failure that is not the gateway's, such as a worker lost mid-step, the image is asked for again and likely paid twice. Every failed image is asked for again in that case today; the waited one is the one likely billed. A fix: keep a waited outcome in the memo, as a decline is kept, so a step run again reads it instead of asking. Pick up before the first live run that makes an image (the Launch checklist), or at the next change to memo.ts's once() or to the asset step's tries, whichever comes first (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23)) |

When a stop or the bound ends the job it cancels it, best effort. A frame that cannot be made, fetched or typed is left out, and the reason goes into the evidence (wire.middleFrame.notMade). - Cost: FAL_VIDEO_EXTRAS (exported) prices both extractions at the 10 s bound in every clip's admission and settled cost, and the advisor estimate sums the same list. Every fal clip now costs $0.002 more, whether or not the middle frame is made, as the row asks. - gateway.ts: stores the frame best effort (left out if it cannot be stored or is not PNG, JPEG or WebP) and returns the optional VideoResult.middleFrameArtifactId and middleFrameMediaType. The mock makes a distinct one. - runner: relayLink announces 'Middle frame of shot N' and records middleFrameArtifactId on the shot; a regeneration without one clears the old one. linkView shows start, middle (when present) and last under captions; LINK_CAPTIONS.middle is 'A frame from the middle of the generated clip:'. - product.c5.shot-check is now v3, and it and the Motion explainer say what is shown. - ADR-0090 has a Later note (2026-10-28). | Row removed (its text: F10 follow-up. | An answer applied by a writer can be replaced before delivery by a from-scratch writer or a later pick (F01 (b)) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/recipe-semantics, 241c12f, a04e55f), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: answersReachingWork now follows the work to the end of the run. The analysis returns what the final work holds, instead of marking an answer reached when it lands. Two things now replace the work. A from-scratch AI writer (a model writer whose prompt never shows the artifact; ASSET, RELAY and skipped steps excluded) leaves the work holding only what it takes in itself. A pick makes the work the option it chooses, holding what the options hold plus what its model judge decided by. A candidate of replicate starts from the outer work; a candidate of each starts from its option. A one-shot gate keeps what its body's work holds, because its critic decides whether the work passes. New refusals (ST12 critique-unapplied): a list applied, then a seed or an intent-only draft; and a pick of candidates made before the fix (the round-9 idx 16404 and 19392 shapes). Docs updated; ADR-0098 Later note. | Row removed (its text: A later AI writer that does not take the artifact in (a seed, or a draft from the intent alone), or a pick of candidates made before the writer, replaces the work, so an admitted answer is never delivered; follow "the work is replaced" in answersReachingWork, or have the composed profile refuse a from-scratch writer after Distill (ADR-0098 residual). Before composed recipes are offered to anyone besides the owner, or before the study registry's first entry, which pins the validator's ruleset (ADR-0059) (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-18)) |

| Recommending and composing hold their web request for as long as the waits allow (B-070) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/web-server, ad1b44f), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: Took the row's first option, a single flight. first-recommendation.ts is generalised to sharedCall(store, key, make), and firstRecommendation now uses it. composeIntake shares one call per intake and model profile, and a re-route (recommendIntake with answers) shares per intake, profile and answers. Once a call settles, the next press asks again, so 'Try building again' still re-composes. Nothing is aborted when the request ends, so paid work is kept. The Recommendation screen now shows a muted note while pending, re-routing or composing: each model call waits as long as Settings' 'How long to wait for an answer' allows (at least 15 minutes for text), and leaving or reloading the page does not stop it. The note gives no total minutes: the true total depends on the advisor's model, repairs and decline retries, so it names the setting instead. | Row removed (its text: The advisor's intake (2 calls, packages/advisor/src/ask.ts) and compose (one call and up to two repairs, compose.ts) run inside the web app's request (apps/web/src/server/api.ts, recommendIntake and composeIntake). byway's web server sets no request timeout (apps/web/src/server/dev-server.ts, requestTimeout 0), and the browser's request has none of its own (apps/web/src/components/api.ts). Once block 2 merges, only each model's chosen wait bounds these calls. Its approved waits design puts a stalled intake at 3 waits (5 with declines) and a stalled compose at 4 (7): 1 to 1.75 hours at the default 15 minutes, and 4 to 7 hours at 60, while the screen shows "Composing…". Steps get a 24-hour backstop. These requests have no bound of byway's own, and block 2 removes Node's five-minute fetch limit that used to cut off a Google call. Nothing stops these calls when the request ends, and that is on purpose: their answers are kept on the intake, and the first recommendation is shared across tabs and reloads (first-recommendation.ts, ADR-0091). So the fix must not abort when the person leaves the page, which would throw away paid work. What goes wrong: if a reload, sleep, a network change or a browser's own limit ends the request first, the screen says byway is not reachable, or shows no recipe yet, while the call is still under way. "Build my own" and a re-route with changed answers have no single flight, so trying again buys a second compose beside the first. Both are paid, and the later one is the one stored. Nobody has checked how long browsers keep such a request open. Possible fixes: let a retry or reload join the call already under way (a single flight per intake for compose and re-route, as firstRecommendation does), or run these calls off the request and have the screen watch the intake. Either way, say on the screen how long it may wait. Since block 2's fix round (0ecc105; CRITIC-1 of block 2's check), a recommend or compose call whose wait ran out says so on the screen, that it may have been billed, and where to change the wait, where it said "The model provider did not answer. Try again in a minute."; the rest of this row stands. Pick up when a live intake or compose runs longer than five minutes, or its screen shows an error while its call is still under way in Settings' spending records, or one "Build my own" pays for two composes; and with the hosted design (row "A hosted byway: a URL other people can open"), since a proxy in front of a hosted app commonly cuts long requests (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-22)) |

ADR-0114 item 3 was corrected to say so ("except four"), but the comment was not, and it is the first place a reader looks for how fal's requests are bounded. Bring it in line with ADR-0114 item 3. No step passes one today; the doc comment says so. The message for a render cut by its deadline already existed in loaded()'s late(). Test changes: (1) 'bounds a page that never settles' checks the renderer's own 5 s limit on a stuck render alone. The plain render now follows a stuck render given a 5 s deadline of its own, on the existing patient renderer (300 s own limit), and checks the deadline message, one context, the context closed first, then the next render. The held-close and cancel part is unchanged. (2) 'a read slower than its deadline' does the held read on a quick reader (own 5 s limit message) and on a patient reader with a deadline (deadline message), then reads Next on the patient one. (3) 'is bounded and cancellable like a render' keeps the quick reader's 2 s own-limit checks for both stuck pages, adds the same pages on a patient reader with a 2 s deadline, then reads Next on the patient one. runNode no longer kills its child after 50 s: the test's own 60 s is the hang guard, and onTestFinished kills the child. Every earlier assertion on the own-limit messages is kept. | page-copy.test.ts's fuzz test draws from the lossy generator product (B-101) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/runner-renderer-tests, 1653363), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: The fuzz generator in page-copy.test.ts now uses Math.imul(seed, 1103515245), and divides by 0x80000000 instead of 0x7fffffff so a draw is always below 1. The old divisor could return exactly 1, and then 'abcde'[5] would be undefined, an edge the `!` hid. Measured with the fixed seed 7: the old lossy generator gave only 479 distinct pairs out of the 2,000 drawn. The exact one gives 1,997. So the effect was much larger than the row's 'likely small'. This is a test-only change. | Row removed (its text: The test "the edit script matches as many words as the table, in order, on 2,000 random pairs of word lists" (packages/runner/test/page-copy.test.ts) draws its word lists from `seed = (seed * 1103515245 + 12345) & 0x7fffffff`. The product passes a double's 53 bits and loses its low bits, the defect ADR-0097's Later note names in the claims oracle's old generator, which now takes the product exactly with Math.imul (scripts/test/claims-oracle/oracle.ts). So the stream may enter a short cycle sooner than intended and try fewer distinct pairs than 2,000; with its fixed seed the effect is likely small. Found off-path by the re-check of 2026-10-21 (REC2-6; docs/adr/evidence/reports/b1-checks-2026-10-21.md). Switch it to Math.imul (a test-only change) at the next change to page-copy.test.ts, or if a page-copy fault turns up that this test should have caught (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-21). | The pre-run estimate plans every brief at 1,500 tokens (B-106) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/advisor-estimate, 5628486, b50d32c), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: The estimate now gets the person's brief. PricingContext.problem sizes every prompt that shows the problem (promptShows on the intent port) with the gateway's textTokens(problemSlot(problem)). Of the planned 1,500 tokens, 1,000 are taken as the template's own words (new INSTRUCTION_TOKENS); a problem longer than the other 500 adds the difference. So short briefs leave every catalog figure unchanged, and a large brief reaches the long-context tier through tokenCost. TokenRule now takes CallSizes {prompt, art, view} instead of the medium. The brief is passed by advisor.ts (recommend, compose, admit), by the Advisor contract's estimate(recipe, profileId, problem?), by the Recommendation page's per-recipe options (intake.problem), and by the Run screen's question figures. So three figures do not grow with the brief: the Recommendation screen's estimate, the question figures (estimateQuestions) and the default run budget (apps/web/src/view/format.ts, suggestBudget, a quarter above the high estimate). The spend cap still holds: the gateway reserves each call on its real prompt (packages/gateway/src/estimate.ts, estimateInputTokens), and since block 2's LC-TIERS it prices that reservation at the call's tier. Block 2 (P2) lets a maximal brief run: 20,000 characters plus five attachments of 200,000, about 255,000 tokens of English. Each call of the fifteen run templates that carry the problem slot (the seed, build, draft, framings, angles, implement and interview ones) then sends 170 to 230 times its planned input, and on Gemini 3.1 Pro it crosses the 200K tier, which the estimate never reaches for that call. On 2026-10-21 at 23:08 Claude told the owner that block 2's builder would check whether the pre-run estimate needs the tiers. The answer is that the estimate prices at the tier but never reaches it for a large brief; ADR-0115's Consequences say so. The fix: give the estimate the brief, and size the problem part of each prompt that carries it with the gateway's textTokens, so the figure, the budget and the tier follow the brief. | Runner tests: Temporal's local test server sometimes starts too slowly under load (B-117) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/test-time, 4869fba), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: Added startOnceMore() and startedTooSlowly() in packages/runner/src/dev-engine.ts. When TestWorkflowEnvironment.createLocal fails with Temporal core's StartupTimeout ("did not start within"), the start is tried exactly once more. I read @temporalio/core-bridge 1.24.0's source: its fixed 5 s wait is 50 connection tries 100 ms apart, and on that failure core kills and awaits the server process itself. - startDevEngine retries on byway's own port only after assertPortFree(port) passes, so the first server is gone. If the port is still taken, the plain port-in-use error is thrown and nothing is started again. - createHarness (test/helpers/harness.ts) names no port, so the retry gets a fresh free port from core. This also covers the 'Only one usage of each socket address' bind failure. New engine-start.test.ts: - a real reproduction of the bind failure: a listener holds the port, the real cached CLI fails to bind it, and the installed SDK's own error text is checked - one retry, then the second miss is thrown with no third try - no retry for any other error - no retry when the port is still taken | Row removed (its text: In gates run under load, whole runner test files failed because the ephemeral Temporal server did not start within 5 s, a wait inside Temporal's SDK (the test hooks allow 120 s). In F07 round 14 these were configurations, controls and liveness; earlier it was real-prompts.test.ts. Reruns passed. D4's recorded CI runs have not hit it, but CI's 2-CPU machines could, and CI's rule (ADR-0103, README) is that a test that fails only sometimes is named and set aside. If it recurs, have the harness try the start once more, or lengthen the wait if Temporal's SDK lets it be set. Timing flakes are named and set aside (the owner's "Not now", 2026-10-18), and Claude's reading of that answer is that such a fix is put to the owner first (ADR-0107 says nothing of it; on 2026-10-21 the owner chose "Fix the test in this batch (Recommended)" for the renderer-check test when asked). It recurred on 2026-10-22, during block 2's build. The W-core builder ran the full runner suite after P2-INPUTS (fix/fx-b2-wcore at fc29bd8, 17:35 local, while the other builders worked in parallel), and controls.test.ts failed because its Temporal dev server did not start within 5 s. Run alone, the file passed 44 of 44, and the builder's later runs did not hit it (configurations.test.ts and controls.test.ts at cfa059a, 63 of 63). That is this row's trigger, so the fix goes to the owner. The options: have createHarness (packages/runner/test/helpers/harness.ts, TestWorkflowEnvironment.createLocal from @temporalio/testing 1.24.0) try the start once more; lengthen the wait, if that SDK lets it be set; or keep the flake named and set aside. It matters now because block 2's gate runs the full suite locally and on both CI machines, and each hit costs a rerun. Put it to the owner with block 2's check results, and build it only on a yes. If the owner keeps it named and set aside, pick it up again when it fails a CI run (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-22). It fired again in block 2's second fix round, and the question had not gone to the owner with block 2's check results (Claude's omission). The gate at 4d71dc2 failed on it twice on 2026-10-23: at 09:59 local controls.test.ts's Temporal test server did not start within 5 s (its 45 tests skipped; 5,660 passed), and on the rerun dev-engine.test.ts's did not (2 tests failed; 5,703 passed). Run alone, both files passed, 49 of 49. The third run passed whole (232 files, 5,705 tests, 58 browser tests). Claude also found that byway's own engine start, startDevEngine in packages/runner/src/dev-engine.ts, calls the same TestWorkflowEnvironment.createLocal, so the same 5 s applies to it. The wait is fixed inside Temporal's compiled core (@temporalio/core-bridge 1.24.0), and the SDK's TypeScript options have no setting for it. On a slow or busy machine byway's start could fail with Temporal's error; nothing is lost, since no run moves while the engine is down and each continues from the engine's saved state at the next start. Asked at 10:02, the owner first wrote at 10:08 "explain to me what this is about truly. Are we handling potential API call failures with Temporal? What are the implications of the choices here?". Asked again at 10:09 "Temporal's 5 s server start: it is about starting byway's engine, not about API calls. It fails tests under load (a rerun fixes it), and since byway's own start uses the same call, a slow machine could fail to start byway (you'd start it again; nothing lost, no money at risk). Which?", the owner chose at 10:10 "Retry once, tests and byway's start, on the follow-up branch (Recommended)", whose description read "When Temporal's server misses its 5 s, byway makes sure that server is gone, then starts it once more: in the test harness and in byway's own engine start (dev-engine.ts), with tests. Built on the follow-up branch after Block 2 merges, before the first live run, and checked there. Until then a gate or CI run that fails only on this start is rerun.", over "Retry once for tests only, on the follow-up branch", "Retry once, tests and byway's start, inside Block 2" and "Keep it as is" (docs/adr/evidence/conversation-continued.md). Build it on the follow-up branch after block 2 merges, before the first live run (the owner's choice, 2026-10-23 10:10); until then a gate or CI run that fails only on this start is rerun. Block 2 merged on 2026-10-23 (65825f4), so it is due on that branch now (said here in the records pass after the merge). A second way the start fails (the sweep of 2026-10-23): in two full runs of packages/runner during block 2's first fix round (2026-10-23, about 02:00 to 02:07 local), a different Temporal-backed file failed each time, real-prompts.test.ts and then dev-engine.test.ts, and the second run's log shows the test server failing to bind its port ("Only one usage of each socket address"), most likely from parallel runs on the machine (the fix round's spend builder's notes). Both files passed alone. Claude's reading is that the owner's "Retry once, tests and byway's start, on the follow-up branch (Recommended)" covers this failure too: the retry makes sure the first server is gone and starts the next on a fresh port, in createHarness and in startDevEngine, with a test for each failure. Covering the bind failure is Claude's addition, said to the owner in that branch's report (trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23)) |

| The CI time row's listed wall-clock limits made time-free on their own branch, before the first live run (B-120) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/test-time, 5d33a65), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: Made every listed wall-clock limit time-free: - page-copy.test.ts BOUND_MS now counts CPU time with process.cpuUsage(), not the wall clock, and SIZE_TEST_MS is gone. A stall or a busy machine does not add CPU time, but this is still a time measure, though not a wall-clock one. - flow-memo.test.ts:73 (1,000 ms): the timing test is replaced by validating a 40-deep nest. Without its memo the walk would be about 2^40 walks and could never finish, so only the hang guard can stop it. The 22-deep count test already pins the memo. - recipe limits.test.ts:95 (500 ms): the timing line is dropped. The walk doubles per level (I checked limits.ts), so a 40-deep nest without the memo can never finish. - spend-book.test.ts:235/:255 (2,000 and 900 ms): a spy on Ledger.withLock records each wait for the ledger's lock. The test asserts at most two waits, each of drainWaitMs. The second test holds its 8 calls in heldAdapter and releases them together. The file's until() lost its 5 s clock bound. - memo.test.ts:175 (2,000 ms): a setTimeout spy asserts the retry pauses are exactly [5, 10, 20] ms. - renderer-check.test.ts: the 13 explicit 60 s timeouts are removed, so the root 60 s guard applies. These tests launch real Chromium, so they cannot be made time-free. - renderer-env.test.ts: spawnSync with 60 s / 120 s became an awaited execFile with no timeout, killed when the test ends (onTestFinished + AbortSignal). - state.spec.ts:296: the CI log shows the real root cause was "route.fetch: read ECONNRESET" after 9.0 s, not the 30 s wait. Playwright 1.63's request context uses a keepAlive agent and reused a socket the server had closed. All three route.fetch calls in the file now go through fetchOwn(), which sends `connection: close` so no socket is ever pooled. The settle after "Sent" waits with timeout: 0, so the test's 6-minute limit is the hang guard. On main's Windows run 40819626580 the "Sent" test (apps/web/e2e/state.spec.ts) died again with ECONNRESET: its `connection: close` header did not stop route.fetch taking a connection from the browser context's pool that Node's server had closed as it idled. The root fix (after the merge): each intercepted request is asked again through a request context made for it alone and disposed once its answer is read, and the test fails the page's refreshes from before the answer reaches byway, so none can see the question settled in the meantime (5 of 5 local runs passed). | Row removed (its text: Also left, any of which can make the next red Windows run: the wall-clock limits in packages/runner/test/page-copy.test.ts (`BOUND_MS = 5000`), packages/validator/test/flow-memo.test.ts:73 (1,000 ms), packages/recipe/test/limits.test.ts:95 (500 ms), packages/gateway/test/spend-book.test.ts:235 and :255 (2,000 and 900 ms) and packages/runner/test/memo.test.ts:175 (2,000 ms); packages/runner/test/renderer-check.test.ts's 13 tests that launch Chromium for real (60 s each; "on, and it starts: ready inside the sandbox, from one launch, closed again" took 40.9 s under the heaviest load the run-start builder made on the owner's machine on 2026-10-21, the narrowest margin in that file) and scripts/test/renderer-env.test.ts (120 s, its child 60 s). None of these is a named flake, so under ADR-0107 item 4 a Windows failure on one stops a risky merge; the 30 s wait for "Sent" in apps/web/e2e/state.spec.ts (it failed once locally under load); and vitest's default 5 s per test, which vitest.config.ts did not raise until block 2's second fix round (below). (packages/runner/test/liveness.test.ts's 10 s limit on the worker's shutdown left this list in the first block's fix round of 2026-10-22, RS-7: the test now checks that the worker stopped with the slow call still in flight, and its 20 s race only stops a shutdown that hangs.) The three renderer-check tests whose start-up probe has 50 s (row "What D2 left (ADR-0105)", item (5)), the renderer tests' other wall-clock limits (row "Wall-clock limits left in the renderer tests"), Temporal's 5 s start and C5's 120 s wait have their own rows (C5's closed on 2026-10-23). | A Pause asked while a step runs, then a budget refusal in that step, wedges the run with no Resume button (B-135) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/engine-workflow, 48d509f), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: ENGINE-2 fix, as the audit verified it. Interpreter.ask() takes an optional 4th argument `how: { underWay?: boolean }` and skips control.boundary() when it is set. askBudget passes { underWay: true }, so a budget question asked from a step already under way no longer waits on a pending pause (which was itself waiting for that step). Every other ask still waits at the boundary. I added a doc comment on ask() and a one-line why at askBudget. RunScreen.tsx is unchanged: the screen shows 'waiting' with the question open, then 'paused' once the person answers. | Row removed (its text: Ponytail audit ENGINE-2 (report item 1; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch A of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix keeps behaviour as it is. ask() waits at the pause boundary before it opens its checkpoint. The budget question is asked from inside act(), inside control.machine(), so the refused step still counts in activeSteps. The step waits for the pause to end. The pause waits for the step to end. Neither ever happens. The status loop keeps writing 'running' (the question was never added to `pending`), so the Run screen shows Pause, not Resume (Resume only appears when status === 'paused'). A second Pause does nothing. The only way out is Cancel, which throws away everything the run has paid for. Case: a C1 or C3 run close to its budget. The person clicks Pause while an asset step, a critic with shuffles, or a model step on its contract retry is running. That step's next call is refused at the budget. No question opens, the run never pauses, and it reads Running until it is cancelled. The same happens when the rerank or a C pick's judge is refused. Fix: interpreter.ts: `async ask(call, spec, extra = {}, how: { underWay?: boolean } = {})`, and replace :290 with `if (!how.underWay) await this.control.boundary();`. In askBudget (:266): `this.ask(call, { kind: 'confirm', prompt: budgetPrompt(amount, b.scope) }, {}, { underWay: true })`. Nothing else changes. This is the exact patch I verified.) |
