The contract doc for clearUncertain in contracts/src/spend.ts now says this. The wrong comment on 'resent' in apps/web/src/view/spending.ts is fixed; that edit fires B-038, which is left to web-ui. One adjacent fix: a press of Clear on such a row would have done nothing you can see. So a new helper, clearable() in view/spending.ts, makes SpendingRecords.tsx show 'You can clear it once the call sent again ends.' in place of Clear for a row whose opId is in records.inFlight, and keeps that row out of 'Clear all N'. ADR-0095 has a dated Later note (2026-10-28) quoting the owner. Test first: the contract suite has 4 cases, one for each way the call ends: abandon 'waited', endLeases, the lease running out, and a second retain followed by settle. They failed 12 of 12 across the three book fixtures before the fix and pass after. | Row removed (its text: Under the external review's F03 (paid calls bought twice or their cost lost): ponytail audit MONEY-1 and DATA-1 (report item 2; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch B of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix changes behaviour or a contract (the owner's yes needed). A row is listed (and can be cleared) as soon as retained_usd > 0, even while its resend is still under way. If that resend then ends with no answer, abandon() moves the row to 'uncertain'. But UNCERTAIN requires cleared_at IS NULL, so the call's reservation is counted nowhere and the row drops out of Settings. Example: an Opus call reserves $0.40. The first stream resets, so $0.40 is retained and the call is sent again. While the second send is streaming, the person presses Clear all. The second send then stalls and is abandoned. The workspace's uncertain total is $0.00; without the clear it is $0.80. view/spending.ts also tells the person that a 'resent' row's call "settled, or was not billed", which is false while it is still in flight. Fix: Smallest (DATA-1's version): add `AND NOT (${IN_FLIGHT})` to the clear statement (spend-book.ts:606-607) and `if (inFlight(r, now)) continue;` to MemorySpendBook.clearUncertain (memory-book.ts:349); the listing stays as it is. The report's version (#2) also adds `AND NOT (${IN_FLIGHT})` to uncertainRows (:603-604) and `&& !inFlight(r, now)` to the records() filter (memory-book.ts:320). Owner's choice: (a) hide the row while its call is in flight. Settings' "may have been billed" total then names an amount that no row lists until the call ends, which can be up to the model's wait. (b) Keep listing it, and Clear does nothing until the call ends. Optionally, SpendingRecords leaves out Clear for a row whose opId is also in records.inFlight. Either way, correct the comment at view/spending.ts:36. That edit to spending.ts fires B-038's trigger. The owner chose at 02:48 on 2026-10-24 "(b) Keep it listed; Clear waits until the call ends (Recommended)" over "(a) Hide it while it is in flight". Both books change in step (batch L, one book, is not now). The comment edit in apps/web/src/view/spending.ts fires row B-038's trigger.) |
| Every admission scans the whole paid-call table twice, because its sweeps cannot use their partial index (B-138) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/store-spend, ff08a8a), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: Each lease sweep's WHERE now starts with state IN ('open', 'sent', 'accepted') AND, so SQLite searches the paid_operations_live partial index instead of scanning the whole table. The SET clauses are unchanged and the same rows are swept. Tests: a new store test captures the two sweep statements from db.prepare and checks with EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN that they use the index; it failed before the fix (SCAN). A new contract case pins what the sweep does: after b's admission sweeps, a late renew from a holder that slept revives none of its open, sent or accepted rows. | Row removed (its text: Ponytail audit DATA-2 (one of the report's smaller findings; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch B of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix keeps behaviour as it is. paid_operations keeps every call ever made (nothing deletes a row), across the web process and the worker. The index made for the sweep, paid_operations_live, is partial (WHERE state IN ('open','sent','accepted')). The sweeps filter on `state = 'open'` and `state IN ('sent','accepted')`, which SQLite does not match to that index, so each sweep is a full SCAN. Assumed load: one person, about 50 calls per run. 100k rows is then roughly 2,000 runs. At that size each admission holds the database write lock, and blocks its own process's event loop, for about 50 ms, 23 ms of it in the two sweeps. Meanwhile the other process's writes wait. The exposure sums stay O(rows) after this fix: workspaceExposure takes 19 ms and spend()'s byRun 34 ms at 100k rows. They are the next thing to grow. Fix: At packages/store/src/spend-book.ts:552-553, add `state IN ('open', 'sent', 'accepted') AND` to the front of each sweep's WHERE. That is a two-line change and the SET clauses stay as they are. The merged CASE statement in the finding was also checked and behaves the same, but it rewrites the SET clauses, so the prefix is the smaller and safer change. It touches money (the spend book), so it gets an adversarial check before push (ADR-0107). Same file as F03 (f); see row B-129.) |
| Nothing tests that admit, markSent, accept and settle commit with synchronous=FULL and then put NORMAL back (B-139) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/store-spend, ff08a8a), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: Tests only, no source change. On a file connection from openDatabase, a spy on db.exec checks that admit, markSent, accept and settle each run exactly FULL, BEGIN IMMEDIATE, COMMIT, NORMAL. A second admit with the same opId runs FULL, BEGIN IMMEDIATE, ROLLBACK, NORMAL. retain, markLedgered and clearUncertain run plain BEGIN IMMEDIATE and COMMIT. PRAGMA synchronous reads 1 at the end. Checked by mutation: with the finally that restores NORMAL removed, the test fails; the source was restored afterwards. | Row removed (its text: Ponytail audit DATA-4 (one of the report's smaller findings; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch B of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix keeps behaviour as it is. No test reads or watches PRAGMA synchronous: grep over apps, packages and scripts finds it only in source files. Several mutations pass all 15 store test files (246 tests) and the contract suite: replacing #durable with #tx in admit or settle, or deleting the finally that restores NORMAL. Without FULL, a power loss after an admitted call was sent can roll back the admission (the WAL commit was not synced). The book then has no row for a billed call, and the caps never count it. Without the restore, every later write on that shared connection fsyncs, which is the kind of stall B-127 and B-129 are measuring. Fix: Add the test only. Per AGENTS.md (ADR-0107's Later notes of 2026-10-23), a tests-only change runs the gate and merges without an adversarial check, even though it guards money.) |
| An attempt Temporal has cancelled still re-sends its call, while the next attempt buys it again (F03 (g)) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/gateway, 12b45aa), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: Once Temporal cancels an attempt (for example a heartbeat timeout while the run goes on), its completion and image retries stop: nothing is sent again, and a retry's wait ends at once. The fix is a small helper, haltOn(stop, cancel), in gateway.ts, used by both completeOnce and imageOnce. It joins stop and operation.cancel with AbortSignal.any, but only for withRetries. stopIfEnding and the adapter's cut-off signal still use the stop alone, as ADR-0111 item 1 requires: a cancel never cuts off a stream already running. The comment on SpendContext.operation.cancel in packages/contracts/src/gateway.ts now says so. ADR-0111 has a Later note dated 2026-10-28 that quotes the owner's 02:48 answer of 2026-10-24 word for word and says the finding and fix are Claude's (from the audit, MONEY-2). | Row removed (its text: Under the external review's F03 (paid calls bought twice or their cost lost): ponytail audit MONEY-2 (report item 3; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch C of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix changes behaviour or a contract (the owner's yes needed). When Temporal cancels an attempt (its 30 s heartbeat timed out after a laptop sleep or a stalled worker, or the worker is shutting down), PaidCalls stops renewing the call's row (paid.ts:280). The next attempt then takes the key over within one lease and buys the call itself. The cancelled attempt's withRetries ignores operation.cancel, though, so a connection reset after the cancel still retains and re-sends, and a 429 still retries. askOnceMore refuses its decline-retry once cancel has fired; the transport re-send follows the opposite rule. Example under the expected load (one person on a laptop): the lid is closed for 2 minutes during an Opus build. On wake, Temporal times out attempt 1 and its heartbeat reply cancels it. The stream resets (ECONNRESET, read as 'maybe'), so attempt 1 retains and re-sends. Attempt 2 takes the key a lease later and buys again while that re-send is streaming. Result: two paid answers plus one send that may have been billed, for one step call. Fix: In completeOnce and imageOnce: `const cancel = req.operation?.cancel; const halt = stop && cancel ? AbortSignal.any([stop, cancel]) : (stop ?? cancel);`, and pass `...(halt ? { stop: halt } : {})` to withRetries in place of `...(stop ? { stop } : {})`. Leave stopIfEnding and the adapter's signal on `stop`. AbortSignal.any is available on Node 24, which engines requires. Add to SpendContext.operation.cancel's comment (contracts/src/gateway.ts:62-63) that once it aborts nothing is sent again and a retry's wait ends. Add a dated Later note to ADR-0099 or ADR-0111 that names the provenance (Claude's, from the audit). The owner chose at 02:48 on 2026-10-24 "Yes, nothing is sent again after a cancel (Recommended)" over "No, keep today's re-sends". It widens the cancel contract; a Later note in ADR-0111 records it.) |
| A ledger line that is just `null` makes the ledger check crash (B-140) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/gateway, 12b45aa), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: Fixed auditLedger crashing on a ledger line that is just null: the check now reads typeof e?.id (gateway.ts). Such a line is now reported as a fault on its line, as the audit's design intends, and nothing else changes. | Row removed (its text: Ponytail audit MONEY-4 (report item 15; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch C of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix keeps behaviour as it is. verify() and absorb are written to treat a line that is valid JSON but not an object (null, an array) as damage to report (ledger.ts:564-565). The spend book also records a fault for such a line, which pauses paid calls. But entries() keeps `null` as an entry, and `typeof e.id` on null throws a TypeError. So the check that should name the line fails. Settings' check request errors. At start, dataDirReport's Promise.all rejects, so serve.ts prints "the data directory could not be checked" and the store and artifact checks are lost too. Example: a ledger.jsonl with one entry followed by a line `null`: verify() returns 'line:2', and auditLedger() rejects with a TypeError. Fix: At gateway.ts:820, use `typeof e?.id === 'string'`.) |

| An image is bought again when its asset step runs again after one of its tries failed (B-142) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/engine-memo, 64e265b, 36c65e6), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: Root cause fixed in memo.ts keyOf/once(), as the audit's ENGINE-1 proposed. keyOf now returns { key, undo }. once() calls undo() just before the final plain `throw e`, so a call that kept nothing gives its repeat number back. The asset step's next try of the same image then goes under the same memo key, and a step run again finds that image instead of buying it again. There is no undo after a kept final decline, a PaidNotKept, a DeclineRetryPending or a kept waited outcome. One change from the audit's fix: undo only steps back while no later ask of the same request has begun (repeats.get(at) === n). Without that guard, a failure that lands while a parallel identical ask is under way would hand that ask's repeat out twice and replay its answer. The header sentence about trying again 'under a new key' is reworded. | Row removed (its text: Ponytail audit ENGINE-1 (report item 4; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch D of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix keeps behaviour as it is. keyOf advances the repeat number before every call, whether or not anything is kept under that key. So a try that fails unbilled uses up n=1 and the next try's image is memoized under n=2. When the whole step runs again, try 1 asks under n=1, finds nothing, and the gateway admits it (the failed row released its operation key), so the image is bought again. The integrate prompt then names a new image id, so that model call is bought again too. Case: C1's asset step, hero image. Try 1 gets an unbilled provider error after the gateway's own retries, and try 2 succeeds. The detail image is then refused at the run budget. The person raises the budget and the step runs again: the hero image is bought a second time and the image paid for first is never used. Fix: memo.ts: keyOf returns `{ key, undo: () => repeats.set(at, n - 1) }`. once() uses `const { key, undo } = keyOf(kind, asked)` and calls undo() just before the final plain `throw e` at :310. Do not undo after a kept final decline or on PaidNotKept. The DeclineRetryPending path ends the attempt anyway. Reword the header sentence about the asset step's retry going 'under a new key'. Ship it with B-045, whose trigger is 'the next change to memo.ts's once()'. Lands with row B-045, whose trigger fires; row B-044's fires too.) |

The runner is unchanged. | Row removed (its text: Ponytail audit WEB-1 (report item 5; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch E of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix keeps behaviour as it is. startRun checks the run budget against what is left of the workspace (budgetProblem) before it asks the runner, including when the request is a replay of a Start that already made a run. By then that run's first call counts as under way, so 'what is left' has gone down. A budget set near the remainder is refused with a 400 saying the budget is more than what is left, even though the run exists. startIdentity treats any error status as a refusal and drops the id. The screen then says to lower the budget or raise the workspace budget. Either way the next Start goes out under a new id and starts a second run of the same problem. That is two lines, with no change to the runner. In the version that skips the budget check only when the resend's budget is the same, not the report's version.) |
| A Windows start from a lowercase drive letter loads the store twice, and the runner's checks on store errors stop matching (B-144) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/scripts-build, 9e4b93e), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: recordResolution (packages/runner/src/activities/human.ts) now matches store errors by name and code (CheckpointWithdrawnError/'withdrawn' becomes the non-retryable StepError; CheckpointResolvedError/'already-resolved' counts as the earlier attempt's success), in memo.ts's style, and the class import is gone. I grepped the runner, apps/web/src and scripts: this was the only class check on a store error. Gateway errors are the one GatewayError class from @byway/contracts, which loads once through the links, or are already matched by name (isHeldKeyError). So the lowercase-drive double load has no other class-identity break. I did not make the row's larger change (serve.ts importing the packages by name with workspace:* root dependencies), and the brief did not ask for it. | Row removed (its text: Ponytail audit BUILD-1 (report item 7; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch F of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix keeps behaviour as it is. On Windows, when byway is started from a current directory whose drive letter is lowercase (c:\...), serve.ts's store module is file:///c:/... and the runner's @byway/store is file:///C:/.... That makes two module copies, each with its own CheckpointResolvedError class. serve.ts creates the store and hands it to the worker, so a store error comes from the c: copy, while human.ts checks against the C: class. Case: recordResolution's first attempt commits the person's answer, then its emit fails, or the worker stops before it reports. The retry gets CheckpointResolvedError, `instanceof` is false, and the error is thrown again instead of counting as the earlier success. Under QUICK_OPTIONS (5 attempts) the activity runs out of attempts and the run fails, though the answer was saved. A CheckpointWithdrawnError is also thrown raw and retried, instead of becoming the non-retryable StepError. ADR-0109 fixed only the env-names symptom of this double load (by moving that state to globalThis). The double load itself is still there. Fix: human.ts:340-342: match by name and code, in memo.ts:143-152's style. `const err = e as { name?: unknown; code?: unknown } \| null; if (err?.name === 'CheckpointWithdrawnError' && err.code === 'withdrawn') throw new StepError(...); if (!(err?.name === 'CheckpointResolvedError' && err.code === 'already-resolved')) throw e;`, then drop the class import. The root-cause fix is a separate, larger change: import the four packages by name in serve.ts, with workspace:* root dependencies, and update serve.test.ts:86 and serve.ts:43.) |
| A second `pnpm start` while byway runs deletes and rebuilds the running app's .next folder before it notices the engine port is taken (B-145) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/scripts-build, 549ad32), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: main() in scripts/serve.ts now awaits assertPortFree on the engine port (same OPS-1 message) and on the web port (its own message, which names PORT), just after reading them. This happens before quietTemporal, the turbopack rmSync, createStore and next build. assertPortFree takes an optional message function and defaults to portInUseMessage. startDevEngine keeps its own check, for a port taken in between. Behaviour change: when a port is taken, the refusal now comes before the data, keys and checked lines rather than after the build. | Row removed (its text: Ponytail audit BUILD-2 (report item 8; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch F of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix keeps behaviour as it is. The dev engine refuses a port that is already in use at once (packages/runner/src/dev-engine.ts:5, portInUseMessage, OPS-1), but serve.ts reads that rejection only after the build has finished. `next build` first cleans apps/web/.next (everything but cache), and that is the folder the first byway's `next start` serves from: start and demo both use the default distDir '.next'. Case: byway is running and the person types `pnpm start` or `pnpm demo` in a second terminal. The running byway's build folder is deleted and rebuilt under it, so its screens fail while the build runs and can keep failing against the new build until it is restarted. Only after the build does the second process print 'Port 7233 on 127.0.0.1 is already in use'. The e2e suite has its own folder for exactly this reason (README: 'so a byway you are running is not rebuilt under you'), but start and demo have no such protection. Fix: At the top of main(), after the ports are parsed and before quietTemporal and rmSync: `await assertPortFree(temporalPort);` (its OPS-1 message is accurate), plus a web-port check with its own message. For example, give assertPortFree an optional message: 'Port <PORT> on 127.0.0.1 is already in use: is byway already running? Stop it, or choose another port with PORT.' Add assertPortFree to the existing import. What remains: two byways from one checkout on different ports still share apps/web/.next; only a lock in the build folder would cover that.) |

Every open question of that run becomes unreachable, though only one artifact is bad. The README's restore section expects this case: the start-up check reports a file that is not at its recorded size, and byway starts anyway. Fix: In artifactInfo, keep the has() check and wrap get(): `let got; try { got = await services.store.artifacts.get(id); } catch (e) { if (e instanceof Error && e.name === 'ArtifactCorruptError') continue; throw e; } const { record, bytes } = got;` (matched by name, as the web does across bundles).) |
| A failed or interrupted artifact write leaves its partial temporary file in artifacts/tmp forever (B-156) | Landed on 2026-10-28 on fix/fx-landnow (from ln/store-spend, 7cb4f73), under the owner's goal of 2026-10-28 and its one adversarial check over the landed set: In #writeAtomically, the existing try { writeFile; sync } finally { close } is now wrapped in a catch. The catch removes the temporary file (rm with force, its own error swallowed) and rethrows the write's original error. The handle is closed before the remove. As the audit advised, there is no start-up sweep of artifacts/tmp. Test first: it.each over writeFile and sync, each made to reject once with ENOSPC through FileHandle's prototype. Both cases failed before the fix (one file left behind each) and pass after. | Row removed (its text: Ponytail audit DATA-5 (one of the report's smaller findings; docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-report.md), batch J of docs/reviews/ponytail-audit-plan.md; valid, and its fix keeps behaviour as it is. The temporary file is removed only when the rename fails. If writeFile or sync throws (a full disk, an I/O error), the partial file stays. A process stopped mid-write leaves one too: serve.ts exits anyway once ENGINE_STOP_DEADLINE_MS passes, and crashes and power loss do the same. Nothing reads, reports or sweeps artifacts/tmp: the start-up check (check.ts) looks only at recorded artifacts. Each such write therefore leaks up to the artifact's size (a clip, a render) into the data directory and into every backup of it. On a full disk the partial file keeps the space the failed write took. The test "leaves no temporary files behind" (artifacts.test.ts:101) covers only the success path. Fix: In #writeAtomically, wrap the existing try { writeFile; sync } finally { close } in `try { ... } catch (e) { await rm(tmp, { force: true }).catch(() => undefined); throw e; }`, so the file is closed first and the original error is the one thrown. Leave out the start-up sweep of artifacts/tmp. Both processes would run it at every start, it could delete the other process's in-progress file after a long laptop sleep, and it only covers crash leftovers. File it separately if wanted. This touches stored data, so it gets an adversarial check before push (ADR-0107).) |

ST7 is an invariant, so no exception relaxes it, and forceComposed drops any parent, so no derivation suspends it. checkAnswer, the only caller of checkComposedProfile, always runs validate first. profile.ts's knobDefs and knobValueOk are line-for-line copies of structural.ts's. COMPOSED-BLOCK can never fire, because StepSchema's block field is z.enum(BLOCK_CODES) and parseRecipe refuses any other value first. STAGE_WORDS repeats @byway/catalog's STAGE_NAMES, which diff.ts already imports. Fix: Preserving variant: export knobDefs and knobValueOk from @byway/validator and import them in profile.ts; delete the dead COMPOSED-BLOCK check; import STAGE_NAMES from @byway/catalog. | Check agents write into the main checkout: a browser tool's folder and a refuter's marker file (B-088) | Closed on 2026-10-23 at the owner's "Brief rules + tripwire, in AGENTS.md (Recommended)" (12:51): the rules and the tripwire are in AGENTS.md's "Checks that clone the repository", and .playwright-mcp/ is in .gitignore; the strays were deleted at the owner's "Delete all three groups now (Recommended)" | Row removed (its text: A check works in its own clone (AGENTS.md's "Checks that clone the repository"), and the rule is that checks never touch the main checkout. Block 2's check left two things there. (1) An untracked folder, .playwright-mcp/. The Playwright MCP browser tool writes page snapshots and console logs into the session's working directory, which is the main checkout, whatever clone the checker works in. Block 2's check (2026-10-22 23:50 to 2026-10-23 01:17 local) left a page snapshot and a console log of the dev server's /api/waits there, most likely from the WEB lens checking Settings' waits, which said it did not delete the folder and that the parent should. A scan found no private details in it. .gitignore ignores only apps/web's Playwright output, so git status shows the folder, a careless git add would commit it, and the next check that uses the tool writes there again. (2) An empty file, .worktrees/refute-STORE-1-1-done (0 bytes, 2026-10-23 00:32), which a refuter of the check's STORE-1 finding wrote there at the minute its e2e engine started; that engine was found running at 03:08 and stopped (row "An interrupted `pnpm e2e` on Windows leaves its engine running"). .gitignore's .worktrees/ hides it. Both are left in place for the owner. The work: the owner removes both, or the agent does on the owner's yes, since they hold only the tools' output; add .playwright-mcp/ to .gitignore under "Tool state", beside .impeccable/; add to AGENTS.md's "Checks that clone the repository" that a check's tools write into its clone or the scratchpad, never the main checkout; and say so in each check's brief. Put the deletion to the owner with the next question, and make the two edits before the follow-up branch's check is briefed, or before the next check that uses a browser tool, whichever comes first (the sweep of 2026-10-23, m-1; trigger proposed by Claude, 2026-10-23)) |
